# RETROSPECTIVE PASS 2 on `7a41b3f` — rounds `#505` `#506` `#507` `#508`

on the round AFTER this one found three errors in my brief each. **Check the premises, not just

## 1. WHAT THE FOUR ROUNDS CLAIM, one line each

* **`#505` sgirergbroundtrip** — `dev_sgi_re.c`: the RGB colour arms are off by one byte position.
  The PUT arm writes three channels at `buf[0..2]` with an explicit pad at `buf[3]`; the GET arm
  read `buf[1..3]`, so an RGB pixel did not survive a round trip.
* **`#506` lcaconfwidth** — `dev_lca.c`: a 64-bit guarded value was printed through `(int)` in a
  terminating diagnostic, so the message could name a value the guard ACCEPTS. Two sites.
* **`#507` alphaswpctxptbr** — `cpu_alpha_palcode.c`: `swpctx` assigned `apcb_ptbr` twice; the
  second write put `new_flags` into it, so `apcb_flags` was never loaded and the next save
  published a never-loaded word.
* **`#508` alphapfnoffset** — `memory_alpha.c`: the page-table walk returned `PFN << pageSizeShift`
  and dropped the within-page offset, so every walked access resolved to the page's first bytes.

**Each shipped with a rung-3 witness measured RED pre-fix and GREEN post-fix with the same
script.** Three of the four gained an extra row because a fix review refused the round — the
witness could not distinguish the fix from a lesser edit.

+	(*return_paddr) = (ALPHA_PTE_TO_PFN(pte3) << pageSizeShift)

+			    warrant is OUTSIDE this function: dev_sgi_gbe.c's

+			    consumer ignores buf[3], not because PUT writes a
+			    zero there; that is what makes this non-circular.
+			    The RGBA and ABGR PUT arms independently agree --
+			    both lay [R,G,B,x] in memory despite opposite
+			    register word orders -- which leaves this GET the
+			    lone container outlier among the 32-bit arms.
+
+			    The low byte stays ZERO on purpose: RGB carries
+			    three channels, so a corrected read of 0xaabbccdd
+			    yields 0xaabbcc00, NOT the source word.  */
+			*color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8);
 			break;
 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:

## 3. WHAT HAS CHANGED SINCE, and it bears on two of these files

**A later round (`#509`) and a probe-helper audit both found things in this territory. I am
telling you because they may bear on whether these four are right, not to steer you:**

* `dev_sgi_re.c`'s GET arms compute `buf[i] << 24` on a promoted `uint8_t` — undefined for a byte
  `>= 0x80`. That is filed separately (`sgirebufshiftub`). **`#505` edits the RGB GET arm.
  Does its correction sit on that UB, and does that change the disposition?**
* An audit found that **five `negate=True` rows across three of this commit's own witnesses went
  GREEN on a NON-OBSERVATION** — `"absent"`, `"died"`, or `0x0`. They were repaired afterwards and
  measured in both directions. **The question for you: does that change what this commit's
  measured HEAD scores MEAN?** The equality siblings reded on those states, so I believe the
  detectors never escaped and only the rows' own notes overclaimed — **check that belief.**

## 4. QUESTIONS

1. **The premise audit.** Is each fix correct as a repair of the defect it names? Is any of the
   four a repair of something that was not broken, or a partial repair presented as complete?
2. **Which of these four would you NOT have shipped, and why?** All four are already in the tree,
   so this is the question that matters. **A pass that ratifies all four has either had four good
   rounds or has stopped arguing, and only a seat can tell me which.**
3. **Do the added COMMENTS claim anything the code does not do?** This project has repeatedly
   shipped a records correction that was itself wrong.
4. **`#508` deliberately KEPT a `// TODO.` line.** `flags` is never read and the protection bits
   are untested, so `return 2` means writable unconditionally. **Was keeping that TODO right, or
   does the round owe a disclosure it did not make?**

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE — say so.

 110: 	    announce this fix exists to make would never fire.

 115: 	    added SGIRE_W_BADMODE = 1 << 10 BELOW this line.  Demoted rather
 116: 	    than deleted, because the REASONING is still why the convention
 117: 	    exists -- and leaving it live would have pointed the next reader at
 118: 	    a taken bit, which is the duplicate-enumerator collision #499
 119: 	    already paid for and warns about four lines up.  The live
 120: 	    next-free claim is on the LAST enumerator, where it belongs.)  */
 121: 	SGIRE_W_PRIM_OP   = 1 << 9,
 122: 
 123: 	/*  sgirandompixel: horrible_getputpixel()'s unimplemented-mode arm.  ITS
 124: 	    OWN BIT, for the reason #498 measured and #500 repeated -- the four
 125: 	    handlers share one malloc'd struct, so a latch shared with another
 126: 	    site is silenced by that site's ordinary traffic and the announce
 127: 	    never fires.  This one matters more than most: the helper is
 128: 	    PER-PIXEL, called inside line, rectangle and tiled-MTE loops, so an
 129: 	    UNLATCHED announce is a per-pixel flood rather than a diagnostic.
 130: 	    The next free bit is 1 << 11; the enum ends HERE on purpose, as
 131: 	    above.  */
 132: 	SGIRE_W_BADMODE   = 1 << 10,

 134: 
 135: struct sgi_re_data {
 136: 	// Rendering engine registers:
 137: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_a[256];
 138: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_b[256];
 139: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_c[256];
 140: 	uint16_t	re_tex[112];
 141: 	// todo: clip_ids registers.
 142: 	uint32_t	re_linear_a[32];
 143: 	uint32_t	re_linear_b[32];
 144: 
 145: 	// Drawing engine registers:
 146: 	uint32_t	de_reg[DEV_SGI_DE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 147: 
 148: 	// Memory transfer engine registers:
 149: 	uint32_t	mte_reg[DEV_SGI_MTE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 150: 
 151: 	/*
 152: 	 *  #497: width-guard decline latches; see the enum above.
 153: 	 *
 154: 	 *  PLACED LAST, DELIBERATELY, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.
 155: 	 *  re_linear_a/b are written at [tlbi] AND [tlbi+1] with tlbi able to
 156: 	 *  reach 31, so index 32 of either array lands on whichever field
 157: 	 *  FOLLOWS it.  An earlier draft of this change put `warned' between
 158: 	 *  re_linear_b and de_reg; measured with offsetof, that made
 159: 	 *  re_linear_b[32] alias `warned' rather than de_reg[0] -- which both
 160: 	 *  falsified the adjacency the two LINEAR site comments cite, and made
 161: 	 *  an over-run SET WARNING LATCH BITS and silence this device's own
 162: 	 *  diagnostics.  Keeping the latch after mte_reg restores the
 163: 	 *  documented adjacency: re_linear_a[32] is re_linear_b[0] and
 164: 	 *  re_linear_b[32] is de_reg[0].
 165: 	 */
 166: 	uint32_t	warned;
 167: };
 168: 
 169: 
 170: /*
 171:  *  horrible_getputpixel():
 172:  *
 173:  *  This routine gets/puts a pixel in one of the tiles, from the perspective of
 174:  *  the rendering/drawing engine. Given x and y, it figures out which tile
 175:  *  number it is, and then finally does a slow read/write to get/put the pixel
 176:  *  at the correct sub-coordinates within the tile.
 177:  *
 178:  *  Tiles are always 512 _bytes_ wide, and 128 pixels high. For 32-bit color
 179:  *  modes, for example, that means 128 x 128 pixels.
 180:  *
 181:  *  For "linear" modes, y is ignored and x is an offset to select which linear
 182:  *  TLB entry to use.
 183:  */
 184: void horrible_getputpixel(int put, struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data* d,
 185: 	int x, int y, uint32_t* color, int mode)
 186: {
 187: 	uint32_t color_mode = mode & DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK;
 188: 	int bufdepth = 1 << ((mode >> 8) & 3); 
 189: 
 190: 	// dst_mode (see NetBSD's crmfbreg.h):
 191: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_A           0x00000000
 192: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_B           0x00000400
 193: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_C           0x00000800
 194: 	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_A           0x00001000
 195: 	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_B           0x00001400
 196: 	uint32_t tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7;
 197: 	int linear = tlb_mode > 3;
 198: 
 199: 	if (!linear && (x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= 2048 || y >= 2048))
 200: 		return;
 201: 
 202: 	int tilewidth_in_pixels = 512 / bufdepth;
 203: 	
 204: 	int tile_nr_x = x / tilewidth_in_pixels;
 205: 	int tile_nr_y = y >> 7;
 206: 
 207: 	unsigned int tile_nr = tile_nr_y * 16 + tile_nr_x;
 208: 
 209: 	y &= 127;
 210: 	int xofs = (x % tilewidth_in_pixels) * bufdepth;
 211: 	int ofs = 512 * y + xofs;
 212: 
 213: 	uint32_t tileptr = 0;
 214: 
 215: 	/*  #100: cases 0-2 index re_tlb_[abc][256] with a guest-derived
 216: 	    tile_nr = (y>>7)*16 + x/tilewidth; bound it (case 4 self-checks).  */
 217: 	if (tlb_mode <= 2 && tile_nr >= 256)
 218: 		return;
 219: 
 220: 	switch (tlb_mode) {
 221: 	case 0:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_a[tile_nr] << 16;
 222: 		break;
 223: 	case 1:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_b[tile_nr] << 16;
 224: 		break;
 225: 	case 2:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_c[tile_nr] << 16;
 226: 		break;
 227: 	case 4:	tile_nr = x >> 12;
 228: 		if (tile_nr >= 32)
 229: 			return;
 230: 		tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[tile_nr] << 12);
 231: 		ofs = x & 4095;
 232: 		// TODO... probably not correct!
 233: 		// printf("tileptr = %08x x = %i y = %i tile_nr = %i ofs = %i\n", tileptr, x, y, tile_nr, ofs);
 234: 		break;
 235: 	default:/*  sgirandompixel: this arm used to assign `*color = random()' -- with
 236: 		    its exit(1) commented out -- so a failed GET handed the CALLER a
 237: 		    random value, and draw_primitive() then wrote it to the destination
 238: 		    in the same loop iteration, since `color' is shared by the XFER GET
 239: 		    and the PUT.  DE_MODE_LIN_B (0x1400) is one of the FIVE modes
 240: 		    crmfbreg.h documents and extracts to tlb_mode 5, so this was
 241: 		    reachable with a DOCUMENTED mode, not only with a junk one.
 242: 		    MEASURED, rung 3: the destination received 0x4b1763a1, 0x2ee4b4df
 243: 		    and 0x7433cdc1 on successive runs of
 244: 		    regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py.
 245: 
 246: 		    NOW DECLINED: `*color' is left alone, so the caller's own
 247: 		    `uint32_t color = fg' stands and what lands is at least
 248: 		    DETERMINISTIC.  The announce is latched because this helper is
 249: 		    per-pixel.
 250: 
 251: 		    WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, filed rather than silently omitted: the
 252: 		    caller STILL PERFORMS its dependent PUT, so a declined GET still
 253: 		    writes fg (row sgirandomskipput -- suppressing it needs a return
 254: 		    value and every GET site changed, which is more than one site);
 255: 		    LIN_B is still not implemented, and the panel refused to endorse
 256: 		    implementing it as LIN_A-with-the-other-array on symmetry alone
 257: 		    (row sgirelinb); and the COLOUR-mode switch below carries the same
 258: 		    random() shape at its own default (row sgirandomcolour).  */
 259: 		if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_BADMODE)) {
 260: 			d->warned |= SGIRE_W_BADMODE;
 261: 			/*  `dst_mode' is kept from the original text although the
 262: 			    parameter is also passed as src_mode on a GET: the probe
 263: 			    anchors on this literal, and rewording it here would be
 264: 			    scope creep AND would move an oracle anchor mid-round,
 265: 			    which is the laundering shape whatever the intent.
 266: 			    Filed as sgirenamedstmode.  */
 267: 			fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented dst_mode %i for "
 268: 			    "horrible_getputpixel (%s), x=%i y=%i; declined "
 269: 			    "(once) ]\n", mode, put ? "put" : "get", x, y);
 270: 		}
 271: 		return;
 272: 	}
 273: 
 274: 	// The highest bit seems to be set for a "valid" tile pointer.
 275: 	if (!(tileptr & 0x80000000)) {
 276: 		//printf("dst_mode %i, tile_nr = %i,  tileptr = 0x%llx\n", dst_mode, tile_nr, (long long)tileptr);
 277: 		//fatal("sgi gbe horrible_getputpixel: unexpected non-set high bit of tileptr?\n");
 278: 		//exit(1);
 279: 		return;
 280: 	}
 281: 	
 282: 	tileptr &= ~0x80000000;
 283: 
 284: 	uint8_t buf[8];		/* #100: bufdepth can be 8 = 1<<((mode>>8)&3) */
 285: 	if (put) {
 286: 		switch (color_mode) {
 287: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_CI:
 288: 			buf[0] = *color;
 289: 			break;
 290: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 291: 			buf[0] = *color >> 24;
 292: 			buf[1] = *color >> 16;
 293: 			buf[2] = *color >> 8;
 294: 			buf[3] = 0;
 295: 			break;
 296: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
 297: 			buf[0] = *color >> 24;
 298: 			buf[1] = *color >> 16;
 299: 			buf[2] = *color >> 8;
 300: 			buf[3] = *color;
 301: 			break;
 302: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR:
 303: 			buf[0] = *color;
 304: 			buf[1] = *color >> 8;
 305: 			buf[2] = *color >> 16;
 306: 			buf[3] = *color >> 24;
 307: 			break;
 308: 		default:buf[0] = random();
 309: 			buf[1] = random();
 310: 			buf[2] = random();
 311: 			buf[3] = random();
 312: 			// TODO.
 313: 			fatal("[ put: color mode = 0x%x ]\n", color_mode);
 314: 		}
 315: 
 316: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
 317: 		    buf, bufdepth, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
 318: 	} else {
 319: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
 320: 		    buf, bufdepth, MEM_READ, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
 321: 
 322: 		switch (color_mode) {
 323: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_CI:
 324: 			*color = buf[0];
 325: 			break;
 326: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 327: 			/*  #505: this read buf[1..3] while the PUT arm above
 328: 			    writes the three channels at buf[0..2] and an
 329: 			    explicit pad at buf[3] -- so an RGB pixel did not
 330: 			    survive its own round trip, shifting one byte left
 331: 			    with a zero fill on every RGB source read.
 332: 
 333: 			    The PUT arm is the authoritative one, and the
 334: 			    warrant is OUTSIDE this function: dev_sgi_gbe.c's
 335: 			    scanout reads the same tile memory back for display
 336: 			    as (buf[0]<<24)+(buf[1]<<16)+(buf[2]<<8)+buf[3],
 337: 			    then CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8 takes R,G,B from bits
 338: 			    31..24, 23..16 and 15..8 -- byte 0 is R and byte 3
 339: 			    is UNUSED.  So the pad sits at buf[3] because the
 340: 			    consumer ignores buf[3], not because PUT writes a
 341: 			    zero there; that is what makes this non-circular.
 342: 			    The RGBA and ABGR PUT arms independently agree --
 343: 			    both lay [R,G,B,x] in memory despite opposite
 344: 			    register word orders -- which leaves this GET the
 345: 			    lone container outlier among the 32-bit arms.
 346: 
 347: 			    The low byte stays ZERO on purpose: RGB carries
 348: 			    three channels, so a corrected read of 0xaabbccdd
 349: 			    yields 0xaabbcc00, NOT the source word.  */
 350: 			*color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8);
 351: 			break;
 352: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
 353: 			*color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8) + buf[3];
 354: 			break;
 355: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR:
 356: 			*color = (buf[3] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[1] << 8) + buf[0];
 357: 			break;
 358: 		default:// Read "raw" 32-bit value:
 359: 			*color = (buf[3] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[1] << 8) + buf[0];
 360: 			fatal("[ get: color mode = 0x%x ]\n", color_mode);
 361: 		}
 362: 	}
 363: }
 364: 
 365: 
 366: /*
 367:  *  SGI "re", NetBSD sources describes it as a "rendering engine".
 368:  */
 369: 
 370: DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re)
 371: {
 372: 	struct sgi_re_data *d = (struct sgi_re_data *) extra;
 373: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 374: 
 375: 	idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 376: 
 377: 	relative_addr += 0x1000;
 378: 
 379: 	/*
 380: 	 *  #497: THE SIX WIDTH GUARDS BELOW USED TO END THE HOST PROCESS.
 381: 	 *
 382: 	 *  A guest choosing an access width is not an emulator error, so each
 383: 	 *  `len != 8' arm now DECLINES the access and announces itself once.
 384: 	 *  Three properties of the shape are load-bearing:
 385: 	 *
 386: 	 *  1. It is an `else if' SPLICE, not an early return.  A `return' here
 387: 	 *     would skip this function's common tail, and `return 0' would be
 388: 	 *     coerced to -1 by cpus/memory_rw.c and raise EXCEPTION_DBE in the
 389: 	 *     guest -- turning a declined access into a guest bus error.
 390: 	 *
 391: 	 *  2. Control therefore reaches the tail, which answers a declined READ
 392: 	 *     with odata (0).
 393: 	 *
 394: 	 *     *** THIS PARAGRAPH ONCE CALLED THAT TAIL DEAD CODE -- "every read arm
 395: 	 *     below still exit(1)s, so odata is never assigned today".  #499 MADE IT
 396: 	 *     LIVE: the unmatched-address catch-all no longer exits, so a declined
 397: 	 *     read now runs the tail and lands a defined 0, and the shipped detector's
 398: 	 *     `rd sent' row exists to pin exactly that.  The six `TODO: read from'
 399: 	 *     arms below DO still exit(1) -- that half is unchanged and is sgirekills. ***
 400: 	 *
 401: 	 *  3. The guards are NOT redundant.  For TLB_A/B/C and TEX the store is a
 402: 	 *     `for (hwi = 0; hwi < len; ...)' loop, so a narrower access simply
 403: 	 *     runs fewer iterations and stays in bounds -- there the guard is
 404: 	 *     modelling fidelity only.  For LINEAR_A and LINEAR_B it is not; see
 405: 	 *     the note at those two sites.
 406: 	 *
 407: 	 *  Only the WIDTH kills change HERE, IN #497.  The six `TODO: read from ...'
 408: 	 *  kills are still untouched.
 409: 	 *
 410: 	 *  *** THE CATCH-ALL IS NO LONGER UNTOUCHED: #499 DE-FATALISED IT and that row
 411: 	 *  is CLOSED.  The sentence is #497's and was true when written; it is left
 412: 	 *  standing with its correction rather than rewritten, because a reader
 413: 	 *  arriving from #497's records needs to see which half moved. ***  The READ
 414: 	 *  arms are
 415: 	 *  `sgirekills`, the CATCH-ALL is `sgireunmapped` (two different rows: one is a
 416: 	 *  direction, the other an unmatched address with no width predicate at all), as is

  44: 	uint64_t *return_paddr, int flags)
  45: {
  46: 	uint64_t base = cpu->cd.alpha.pcb.apcb_ptbr << ALPHA_PAGESHIFT;
  47: 	uint64_t userMode = cpu->cd.alpha.ps & ALPHA_PSL_USERMODE;
  48: 	// uint64_t vptptr = cpu->cd.alpha.vptptr;
  49: 	
  50: 	// TODO: From the Alpha "arm_book.book":
  51: 	// IF {SEXT(VA<63:VA_SIZE>) NEQ SEXT(VA<VA_SIZE-1>} THEN
  52: 	// 	{initiate Access Violation fault}
  53: 	// IF (VIRBND in use) THEN
  54: 	//   IF (VA LTU VIRBND) THEN
  55: 	//     ptbr_value <- PTBR
  56: 	//   ELSE
  57: 	//     ptbr_value <- SYSPTBR
  58: 	// ELSE
  59: 	//   ptbr_value <- PTBR
  60: 
  61: 	/*  Kernel direct-mapped space (OSF/1, NetBSD):  */
  62: 	if (!userMode && (vaddr >= ALPHA_K0SEG_BASE && vaddr <= ALPHA_K0SEG_END)) {
  63: 		*return_paddr = vaddr & 0x000003ffffffffffULL;
  64: 		return 2;
  65: 	}
  66: 
  67: 	/*
  68: 	 *  UGLY hack for now:
  69: 	 *
  70: 	 *  Before virtual memory has been set up, ptbr is most likely zero.
  71: 	 *  In that case, simply map everything vaddr == paddr.
  72: 	 */
  73: 	if (!userMode && base == 0) {
  74: 		*return_paddr = vaddr & 0x000003ffffffffffULL;
  75: if (vaddr == 0xfffffffd80000000ULL) {
  76: 	fatal("Alpha kernel virtual memory seems to want to be used. Not yet implemented.\n");
  77: 	exit(1);
  78: }
  79: #if 1
  80: 		if ((vaddr & ~0x7fff) == 0x0000000010000000ULL)
  81: 			*return_paddr = (vaddr & 0x7fff) + HWRPB_PADDR;
  82: 
  83: 		if ((vaddr & ~0xffffff) == 0xfffffe0000000000ULL)
  84: 			*return_paddr = 0x7efa000 + (vaddr & 0xffffff);
  85: 
  86: 		/*  At 0x20000000, NetBSD stores 8KB temp prom data  */
  87: 		if ((vaddr & ~0x1fff) == 0x0000000020000000ULL)
  88: 			*return_paddr = (vaddr & 0x1fff) + PROM_ARGSPACE_PADDR;
  89: #endif
  90: 		return 2;
  91: 	}
  92: 
  93: 	// debug("base = 0x%016"PRIx64"\n", base);
  94: if (vaddr == 0xfffffffd80000000ULL) fatal("AYONA3\n");
  95: 
  96: 	uint64_t addr, pte1, pte2, pte3;
  97: 	int i1, i2, i3;
  98: 	unsigned char *pt_entry_ptr;
  99: 	const int pageSizeShift = 13;
 100: 	// const int pageSize = 1 << pageSizeShift;
 101: 	const int bitsPerLevel = 10;
 102: 	const int maskPerLevel = (1 << bitsPerLevel) - 1;
 103:

 105: 	i2 = (vaddr >> (pageSizeShift + 1 * bitsPerLevel)) & maskPerLevel;

 134: 	if (pt_entry_ptr == NULL)

 137: 	pte2 = *(uint64_t *)(pt_entry_ptr);
 138: 	pte2 = LE64_TO_HOST(pte2);

 141: 	if (!(pte2 & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)) {
 142: 		// TODO:
 143: 		// IF level2_pte<KRE> EQ 0 THEN
 144: 		//   {initiate Access Violation fault}
 145: 		// ELSE

 147: 
 148: 		fatal("TODO: pte2 not valid.\n");
 149: 		goto not_found;
 150: 	}
 151: 
 152: 	addr = ((pte2 >> 32) << ALPHA_PAGESHIFT) + (i3 * sizeof(uint64_t));
 153: 
 154: 	pt_entry_ptr = memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(cpu->mem, addr, 0);
 155: 	if (pt_entry_ptr == NULL)
 156: 		goto not_found;
 157: 
 158: 	pte3 = *(uint64_t *)(pt_entry_ptr);
 159: 	pte3 = LE64_TO_HOST(pte3);
 160: 
 161: 	// debug("pte3 = 0x%016"PRIx64"\n", pte3);
 162: 
 163: 	if (!(pte3 & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)) {
 164: 		fatal("TODO: pte3 not valid.\n");
 165: 		goto not_found;
 166: 	}
 167: 
 168: 	/*  #508: was just `PFN << pageSizeShift' -- a PAGE BASE.  memory_rw then takes
 169: 	    `offset = paddr & offset_mask', which was therefore always zero, so every
 170: 	    walked access resolved to the page's first bytes.  Measured: a cold guest ldq
 171: 	    of vaddr+8 returned the vaddr+0 value.  A debugger watchpoint on a walked page
 172: 	    landed at the base for the same reason.
 173: 	
 174: 	    The mask is derived from pageSizeShift rather than written as 0x1fff: this
 175: 	    function already has TWO names for 13 (ALPHA_PAGESHIFT and this local), and a
 176: 	    literal beside the shift is how the two drift.  `|' rather than `+' because
 177: 	    that is what every other CPU's walk in this tree writes; here they are equal,
 178: 	    the shifted PFN having bits 12:0 clear.  The TODO below STAYS: `flags' is
 179: 	    still ignored, the protection bits untested, and `return 2' still reports
 180: 	    every walked page writable.  */
 181: 	(*return_paddr) = (ALPHA_PTE_TO_PFN(pte3) << pageSizeShift)
 182: 	    | (vaddr & ((1ULL << pageSizeShift) - 1));

 184: 	// TODO.
 185: 	return 2;

 187: not_found:
 188: 	/*  No match.  */
 189: 	/*  #194: (Codex/Fable) a failed guest page-table walk must not
 190: 	    abort()/exit() the host; report no-translation (0) so the caller
 191: 	    raises the fault (keeps the emulator alive and debuggable).  */
 192: 	fatal("[ alpha_translate_v2p: 0x%016" PRIx64" wasn't found ]\n", vaddr);

 152: DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_isa)
 153: {
 154: 	unsigned int ofs, i;
 155: 	uint8_t byte;
 156: 
 157: 	relative_addr >>= 5;
 158: 
 159: 	ofs = relative_addr & 3;
 160: 	if (ofs > len) {
 161: 		fatal("[ ofs=%i len=%i in lca_isa access function. "
 162: 		    "aborting ]\n", ofs, len);
 163: 		exit(1);
 164: 	}
 165: 
 166: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 167: 		byte = data[ofs % len];
 168: 		return cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE +
 169: 		    relative_addr, &byte, 1, writeflag, CACHE_NONE);
 170: 	}
 171: 
 172: 	cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, LCA_ISA_BASE + relative_addr,
 173: 	    &byte, 1, MEM_READ, CACHE_NONE);
 174: 
 175: 	for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 176: 		data[i] = i == ofs? byte : 0x00;
 177: 
 178: 	return 1;
 179: }
 180: 
 181: 
 182: DEVICE_ACCESS(lca_ioc)
 183: {
 184: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 185: 	struct lca_data *d = (struct lca_data *) extra;
 186: 
 187: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 188: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 189: 
 190: 	switch (relative_addr + LCA_IOC_BASE) {
 191: 
 192: 	case LCA_IOC_BASE:
 193: 		/*  Ignore? Linux reads from the base at startup.  */
 194: 		break;
 195: 
 196: 	case LCA_IOC_CONF:
 197: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ) {
 198: 			odata = d->ioc_conf;
 199: 		} else {
 200: 			d->ioc_conf = idata;
 201: 			/*  Only bit 0 is implemented so far, the PCI bus 0 vs
 202: 			    bus non-0 selection bit.  */
 203: 			if (idata & ~1) {
 204: 				/*  #506: was `0x%x', (int)idata -- a 64-bit guarded value
 205: 				    narrowed to 32 in the KILL diagnostic, so a write of
 206: 				    0x100000000 trips `idata & ~1' and then reports "0x0",
 207: 				    which is a value this same guard ACCEPTS.  The five
 208: 				    sibling arms below already print the full width this
 209: 				    way; this arm was the outlier among the VALUE-guard
 210: 				    diagnostics.  (Not the file's only narrowing site --
 211: 				    the default arm below has the same cast, and this
 212: 				    round fixes it too.)  */
 213: 				fatal("TODO: Write to unimplemented bit of"
 214: 				    " IOC_CONF: 0x%" PRIx64 "\n", (uint64_t)idata);
 215: 				exit(1);
 216: 			}
 217: 		}
 218: 		break;
 219: 
 220: 	case LCA_IOC_TBIA:
 221: 		/*  TLB Invalidate All.  */
 222: 		/*  TODO: For now, let's just ignore it.  */

GXEMUL-SEC/regress/sgi_re_rgb_probe.py
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: #
   3: #  sgirergbroundtrip -- RUNG-3 WITNESS for dev_sgi_re.c horrible_getputpixel()'s RGB arms.
   4: #
   5: #  COPIED from regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py, whose session already drives an XFER copy
   6: #  through this helper on an unmodified `-E sgi -e ip32'.  Only the SOURCE COLOUR TYPE changes.
   7: #
   8: #  THE DEFECT: the RGB PUT and GET arms disagree about which byte holds which channel.
   9: #      PUT  buf[0] = c>>24;  buf[1] = c>>16;  buf[2] = c>>8;  buf[3] = 0;
  10: #      GET  *color = (buf[1]<<24) + (buf[2]<<16) + (buf[3]<<8);
  11: #  PUT writes the top three bytes at 0,1,2; GET reads 1,2,3.  RGB is the ONLY multi-byte mode
  12: #  that fails its own round trip -- RGBA and ABGR both round-trip, using OPPOSITE byte orders,
  13: #  which is why this is an internal inconsistency and not an endianness question.
  14: #
  15: #  WHY THE ORACLE IS A VALUE AND NOT AN INEQUALITY: an XFER copy GETs with src_mode and PUTs
  16: #  with dst_mode, which are INDEPENDENT guest registers.  With src RGB and dst RGBA the
  17: #  corruption is a computable one-byte left shift with a zero fill, so the row asserts an exact
  18: #  value on both the defective and the fixed build.  No uninitialised byte is read, so the
  19: #  `separate automatic objects have no architectural promise' objection that killed a
  20: #  neighbouring witness design does not apply here -- checked, not assumed.
  21: #
  22: #  (original header follows)
  23: #  sgirandompixel -- the probe this was copied from.
  24: #
  25: #  THE DEFECT.  horrible_getputpixel() extracts `tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7' and switches on it.
  26: #  Cases 0, 1, 2 and 4 are implemented.  The `default:' arm prints, has its exit(1) COMMENTED OUT,
  27: #  and then executes `*color = random(); return;'.  So a failed GET does not decline -- it hands the
  28: #  caller a random 32-bit value.  In draw_primitive() the failed source GET and the destination PUT
  29: #  share one `color' variable in one loop iteration:
  30: #
  31: #      if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN)
  32: #              horrible_getputpixel(0, ..., src_x, src_y, &color, src_mode);   // may set random()
  33: #      ...
  34: #      if (draw)
  35: #              horrible_getputpixel(1, ..., x, y, &color, dst_mode);           // writes it
  36: #
  37: #  AND THE UNHANDLED VALUE IS A DOCUMENTED MODE.  src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h defines five:
  38: #    DE_MODE_TLB_A 0x0000 -> 0 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_A 0x1000 -> 4 handled
  39: #    DE_MODE_TLB_B 0x0400 -> 1 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_B 0x1400 -> 5 *** default -> random() ***
  40: #    DE_MODE_TLB_C 0x0800 -> 2 handled
  41: #
  42: #  CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this witness catches and what it does NOT:
  43: #    CATCHES   the RGB GET arm re-indexing: `rgb corrupt' pins the EXACT post-GET value, so a
  44: #              destination receives anything other than a value distinct from BOTH the source and
  45: #              the pre-painted destination.  Restoring `exit(1)' there kills the machine and reds
  46: #              fix that merely changes the value without correcting the byte positions still reds.
  47: #              ONLY if the pre-paint is removed -- which is why the destination is PRE-PAINTED with a
  48: #              known value and the row rejects it: "left alone" and "wrote random" are then distinct.
  49: #    DOES NOT  establish that any real driver selects LIN_B.  It appears only in crmfbreg.h and the
  50: #              function's own comment; the PROM's MTE zero-fill path does not call this function; and
  51: #              there is no O2/sgimips image in the tree to boot.  **Documented and storable is NOT
  52: #              driver-selected**, and this probe claims only the former (grok, the witness panel).
  53: #
  54: #  DESIGN CREDIT AND THE FALSE NEGATIVE IT AVOIDS (grok, panel_20260906_224008).  The obvious probe --
  55: #  set the source mode to LIN_B, draw, read the destination -- FINDS NOTHING.  At reset the
  56: #  destination mode is TLB_A and re_tlb_a[] has bit 31 clear, so the PUT returns WITHOUT WRITING and
  57: #  the destination is unchanged: a false negative that looks like a clean run.  Using LIN_A as the
  58: #  DESTINATION closes it, because case 4 forces the valid bit:
  59: #      tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[x >> 12] << 12);  ofs = x & 4095;
  60: #  so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10 puts the tile at physical 0x10000, and the byte offset is x itself.
  61: #
  62: #  THE ORACLE IS CERTAIN, NOT PROBABILISTIC.  glibc random() is 31-bit, so the value written has bit
  63: #  31 CLEAR and can never equal a source of 0xAABBCCDD.  Inequality is guaranteed, not 2^-32.
  64: #
  65: #  usage: sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
  66: import os, pty, re, select, sys, time
  67: 
  68: BIN   = sys.argv[1]
  69: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
  70: DUMMY = "/tmp/dummy_sgirand.bin"
  71: if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
  72:     with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
  73:         f.write(b"\0" * 16)
  74: 
  75: CODE      = 0x80001000
  76: SCRATCH   = 0xa0001800
  77: LIVE_VAL  = 0x5a5a5a5a
  78: DE_STATUS = 0xb5004000
  79: DE_STAT   = "0x1e000000"
  80: 
  81: #  CRIME base is 0xb5000000: CRIME_DE_STATUS is 0x4000 and the shipped width probe reads 0xb5004000.
  82: RE_LINEAR_A   = 0xb5001700
  83: DE_MODE_SRC   = 0xb5002000
  84: DE_MODE_DST   = 0xb5002008
  85: DE_DRAWMODE   = 0xb5002018
  86: DE_XVERTEX_0  = 0xb5002070
  87: DE_XVERTEX_1  = 0xb5002074
  88: DE_XFER_ADDR  = 0xb50020a0
  89: DE_FG         = 0xb50020d0        # CRIME_DE_FG -- `uint32_t color = fg' is what a declined GET leaves
  90: DE_XFER_STEPX = 0xb50020a8
  91: DE_PRIM_START = 0xb5002860        # CRIME_DE_PRIMITIVE 0x2060 | CRIME_DE_START 0x0800
  92: 
  93: MODE_LIN_A = 0x1220               # LIN_A 0x1000 | BUFDEPTH_32 0x200 | TYPE_RGBA 0x20
  94: MODE_RGB   = 0x1210               # LIN_A 0x1000 | BUFDEPTH_32 0x200 | TYPE_RGB  0x10
  95: #  The ONE nibble that differs from MODE_LIN_A.  dst stays MODE_LIN_A (RGBA) because setup()
  96: #  hardcodes DE_MODE_DST and takes only src_mode as its parameter -- so this changes the
  97: #  observation path WITHOUT moving the accept control, which is what made the neighbouring
  98: #  row's witness objection inapplicable.
  99: #
 100: #  PREDICTED (pre-fix), computed before the run:  source bytes aa bb cc dd
 101: #    GET RGB reads buf[1..3]   -> color = 0xbbccdd00
 102: #    PUT RGBA writes buf[0..3] -> bb cc dd 00
 103: #    destination reads back    -> 0xbbccdd00   (a correct GET would give 0xaabbccdd)
 104: RGB_CORRUPT = 0xbbccdd00           # what the SHIPPED GET produces
 105: RGB_FIXED   = 0xaabbcc00           # what a CORRECTED GET produces
 106: MODE_RGB64 = 0x1310               # LIN_A 0x1000 | BUFDEPTH_64 0x300 | TYPE_RGB 0x10
 107: MODE_RGB_TLBA = 0x0210            # TLB_A 0x0000 | BUFDEPTH_32 0x200 | TYPE_RGB 0x10
 108: MODE_RGB_TLBA64 = 0x0310          # TLB_A 0x0000 | BUFDEPTH_64 0x300 | TYPE_RGB 0x10
 109: #  *** A TILED SOURCE, AND IT EXISTS TO CLOSE A MEASURED FLOOR BREACH. ***  `tlb_mode = (mode>>10)&7'
 110: #  is 0 here, so `linear = tlb_mode > 3' is FALSE -- which is the whole point.  Every other row in
 111: #  this file drives LIN_A (tlb_mode 4), so `linear' was TRUE in all of them and a repair conditioned
 112: #  on it was invisible: mutant M12_linear scored 8/8, a clean sweep.
 113: RE_TLB_A   = 0xb5001000           # CRIME_RE_TLB_A 0x1000, same base as RE_LINEAR_A's 0x1700
 114: TLBA_TILE  = 0x8002               # re_tlb_a[0].  *** BIT 15 IS NOT DECORATION. ***
 115: TLBA_PHYS  = (TLBA_TILE << 16) & ~0x80000000   # -> 0x20000
 116: #  *** A TILED TILE POINTER MUST CARRY ITS OWN VALID BIT, AND LIN_A'S DOES NOT TELL YOU THAT. ***
 117: #  The helper gates on it before using tileptr at all:
 118: #
 119: #      // The highest bit seems to be set for a "valid" tile pointer.
 120: #      if (!(tileptr & 0x80000000))
 121: #              return;                       <- silent decline
 122: #      tileptr &= ~0x80000000;
 123: #
 124: #  LIN_A sets the bit EXPLICITLY -- `tileptr = 0x80000000 | (re_linear_a[n] << 12)' -- so a probe
 125: #  built from the linear path never has to know.  TLB_A takes it from the STORED VALUE:
 126: #  `tileptr = re_tlb_a[n] << 16', and re_tlb_a is uint16_t, so bit 31 of tileptr is bit 15 of the
 127: #  register.  `re_tlb_a[0] = 2' yields 0x00020000, the guard rejects it, and the GET returns without
 128: #  touching *color -- which leaves the caller's `fg' behind.
 129: #
 130: #  MEASURED TWICE: the row read 0x77665544 = FG_VAL, which this probe's own DE_FG comment names as
 131: #  what a declined GET leaves.  The first correction (a halfword-vs-word shift) was a real bug and
 132: #  fixed a real thing; it was not THIS thing, and the row said so again.  **Two wrong guesses, two
 133: #  identical red rows, and the value never once looked like a tiled pixel.**
 134: #  Tiled addressing is NOT the linear formula: `ofs = 512*y + (x % tilewidth)*bufdepth' rather than
 135: #  `ofs = x & 4095'.  With a 1-wide, 4-tall rectangle the four source rows are 512 bytes apart, so
 136: #  all four are painted -- unlike the LIN_A rows, which collide on one byte by design.
 137: TLBA_ROWS  = [0xa0000000 + TLBA_PHYS + 512 * i for i in range(4)]
 138: #  The SAME colour type at a DIFFERENT TRANSFER WIDTH.  bufdepth = 1 << ((mode>>8)&3),
 139: #  so 0x300 gives 8 where 0x200 gives 4.  buf[0..2] are filled either way, so the expected
 140: #  value is unchanged -- which is exactly what makes it a clean second width to test.
 141: #
 142: #  *** THE FIXED VALUE IS NOT THE SOURCE VALUE, AND AN EARLIER DRAFT OF THIS FILE ASSUMED IT
 143: #  WAS.  That row would have gone RED against a CORRECT fix. ***
 144: #
 145: #  RGB is a THREE-channel mode.  A corrected GET reads buf[0..2] and leaves the low byte of
 146: #  *color at zero -- there is no fourth channel to carry, and PUT's own `buf[3] = 0' says so
 147: #  from the other side.  So a source word 0xaabbccdd copies as:
 148: #
 149: #      source memory      aa bb cc dd
 150: #      GET RGB (fixed)    (aa<<24)+(bb<<16)+(cc<<8)  =  0xaabbcc00      <- dd is DROPPED
 151: #      PUT RGBA           aa bb cc 00
 152: #      destination        0xaabbcc00
 153: #
 154: #  Caught on pass 1 by FOUR seats independently naming 0xaabbcc00 -- grok, glm, the kimi
 155: #  substitute and codex -- against a brief that had stated 0xaabbccdd.  **The dropped byte is
 156: #  CORRECT BEHAVIOUR, not residual corruption**, which is exactly the distinction a row
 157: #  asserting the source value could never draw.
 158: DRAWMODE   = 0x002000f8           # XFER_EN 0x200000 | PLANEMASK 0x80 | BYTEMASK 0x78
 159: PRIM_RECT  = 0x03020000           # DE_PRIM_RECTANGLE 0x03000000 | DE_PRIM_TB 0x00020000
 160: 
 161: TILE_PHYS  = 0x10000              # re_linear_a[0] = 0x10 -> (0x10 << 12)
 162: DST_KSEG1  = 0xa0000000 + TILE_PHYS          # x = 0    -> ofs 0
 163: SRC_OFS    = 0x40
 164: SRC_KSEG1  = 0xa0000000 + TILE_PHYS + SRC_OFS  # x = 0x40 -> ofs 0x40
 165: SRC_VAL    = 0xaabbccdd           # bit 31 SET: random() (31-bit) can never equal it
 166: DST_PAINT  = 0x11111111           # pre-paint, so "left untouched" is distinguishable
 167: FG_VAL     = 0x77665544           # DISTINCTIVE and NON-ZERO on purpose: with fg = 0 the rows
 168:                                   # "left *color untouched" and "zeroed *color" are the SAME
 169:                                   # observation and the oracle is vacuous by construction.
 170: 
 171: OP_NOP      = 0x00000000
 172: OP_B_SELF   = 0x1000ffff
 173: OP_SW_T0_A3 = 0xace80000          # sw $t0, 0($a3)
 174: OP_SD_T0_A3 = 0xfce80000          # sd $t0, 0($a3)
 175: OP_LW_V0_AT = 0x8c220000          # lw $v0, 0($at)
