 177: rows = []
 178: def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
 179:     ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
 180:     rows.append((name, ok))
 181:     print("%-16s %-12s %-12s %-44s %s" % (name, str(got), ("NOT " if negate else "") + str(want),
 182:                                           note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
 183:     return ok
 184: 
 185: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.6):
 186:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 187:     if pid == 0:
 188:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64",
 189:                        "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])
 190:         os._exit(127)
 191:     buf = ""
 192:     def rd(t):
 193:         nonlocal buf
 194:         end = time.time() + t
 195:         while time.time() < end:
 196:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 197:             if not r:
 198:                 continue
 199:             try:
 200:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 201:                 if not d:
 202:                     return False
 203:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 204:             except OSError:
 205:                 return False
 206:         return True
 207:     alive = rd(settle)
 208:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 209:         try:
 210:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 211:         except OSError:
 212:             alive = False
 213:             break
 214:         if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.22):
 215:             alive = False
 216:             break
 217:     try:
 218:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
 219:     except OSError:
 220:         pass
 221:     try:
 222:         os.close(fd)
 223:     except OSError:
 224:         pass
 225:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive
 226: 
 227: def printed(t, reg):
 228:     #  MASK TO 32 BITS.  `lw' sign-extends into a 64-bit register, so a value with bit 31 set
 229:     #  reads back as 0xffffffffaabbccdd and a row comparing raw strings fails on FORMATTING
 230:     #  rather than on behaviour -- measured on this probe's first run.  The shipped
 231:     #  sgi_re_width_probe.py carries the same normalisation for the same reason.
 232:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 233:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % (int(m.group(1), 16) & 0xffffffff)
 234: 
 235: class Addr(object):
 236:     def __init__(self, base): self.a = base
 237:     def take(self, n):
 238:         got = self.a; self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20; return got
 239: 
 240: def run_ops(ops, at):
 241:     put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
 242:     put += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
 243:             "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP)]
 244:     return put + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(ops)]
 245: 
 246: def store(A, addr, val, op=OP_SW_T0_A3):
 247:     return ["a3=0x%x" % addr, "t0=0x%x" % val] + run_ops([op], A.take(1))
 248: 
 249: SENTINEL = 0xdeadbeef
 250: def load(A, addr):
 251:     return ["at=0x%x" % addr, "v0=0x%x" % SENTINEL] + run_ops([OP_LW_V0_AT], A.take(1))
 252: 
 253: def setup(A, src_mode, dst_mode=None, stepx=4, tiled_src=False):
 254:     """Program the engine, paint source and destination, then fire one rectangle.
 255:        Every store is a REAL GUEST INSTRUCTION through memory_rw -- no device_add,
 256:        no debugger `put' to a device register."""
 257:     #  *** THE 64-BIT STORE WRITES THE HIGH WORD TO [n] AND THE LOW WORD TO [n+1]. ***  Measured:
 258:     #  `sd' of 0x10 gave `re_linear_a[0] = 0, [1] = 0x10', so the tile pointer was 0 and every pixel
 259:     #  went to PHYSICAL ADDRESS 0 -- the destination stayed at its pre-paint and the ACCEPT control
 260:     #  failed, which is precisely the "do not leave it 0: that writes phys 0" trap grok named.  The
 261:     #  value below puts 0x10 in the HIGH word so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10.
 262:     c  = store(A, RE_LINEAR_A, 0x0000001000000000, op=OP_SD_T0_A3)  # sd: a 4-byte sw is DECLINED here
 263:     c += store(A, DST_KSEG1, DST_PAINT)
 264:     c += store(A, SRC_KSEG1, SRC_VAL)
 265:     if tiled_src:
 266:         #  *** re_tlb_a IS uint16_t[256] AND ITS STORE LOOP IS PER-HALFWORD, WHICH IS NOT
 267:         #  RE_LINEAR_A'S SHAPE.  Getting this wrong is what the row caught on its first run. ***
 268:         #      for (hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += 2)
 269:         #              d->re_tlb_a[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
 270:         #  So an 8-byte store fills re_tlb_a[0..3] from FOUR big-endian halfwords, and the one
 271:         #  that lands in [0] is bits 63..48.  `TLBA_TILE << 32' -- the shift RE_LINEAR_A needs,
 272:         #  because re_linear_a is uint32_t -- puts the value in re_tlb_a[2] and leaves [0] at
 273:         #  zero, giving tileptr 0 and a DECLINED get.  Measured: the row read FG_VAL
 274:         #  (0x77665544), which this probe's own DE_FG comment names as what a declined GET
 275:         #  leaves.  **The row reported my setup error instead of passing quietly.**
 276:         c += store(A, RE_TLB_A, TLBA_TILE << 48, op=OP_SD_T0_A3)
 277:         for a in TLBA_ROWS:
 278:             c += store(A, a, SRC_VAL)
 279:     c += store(A, DE_MODE_DST,   MODE_LIN_A if dst_mode is None else dst_mode)
 280:     c += store(A, DE_MODE_SRC,   src_mode)
 281:     c += store(A, DE_DRAWMODE,   DRAWMODE)
 282:     c += store(A, DE_XFER_ADDR,  0 if tiled_src else SRC_OFS)   # linear: src_x = addr_src (a BYTE
 283:                                                       # offset).  Tiled: src_x is a PIXEL
 284:                                                       # coordinate, so 0 is the tile's origin.
 285:     c += store(A, DE_XFER_STEPX, stepx)                # must equal src bufdepth or the XFER arm warns
 286:     c += store(A, DE_FG,         FG_VAL)               # what a declined GET leaves in `color'
 287:     c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_0,  0x00000000)           # (x1,y1) = (0,0)
 288:     #  *** (x2,y2) = (0,3): ONE COLUMN, FOUR ROWS.  The height is load-bearing and the width must
 289:     #  NOT be used instead. ***  (The latch row that needed the height lives in the probe this
 290:     #  file was copied from, not here -- but the geometry is kept so the two sessions stay
 291:     #  comparable, and because LIN_A addressing is `ofs = x & 4095' either way.
 292:     #  is the announcement COUNT -- which cannot vary at all if the rectangle is one pixel, because an
 293:     #  unlatched announce then also fires exactly once.  Measured: with (0,0)-(0,0) the M_NOLATCH
 294:     #  mutant scored 8/8, a clean escape, and the row was VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION.
 295:     #  It is the HEIGHT and not the width because LIN_A addressing is `ofs = x & 4095' -- y is
 296:     #  ignored -- so four ROWS write the same byte four times (the value oracle survives) while four
 297:     #  COLUMNS would write overlapping bytes at ofs 0..3 and destroy it.
 298:     c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_1,  0x00000003)           # (x2,y2) = (0,3): 4 pixels, all at ofs 0
 299:     c += store(A, DE_PRIM_START, PRIM_RECT)            # PRIMITIVE|START fires the draw
 300:     return c
 301: 
 302: print("sgirergbroundtrip RUNG-3 WITNESS -- binary=%s label=%s" % (BIN, LABEL))
 303: print("%-16s %-12s %-12s %-44s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))
 304: 
 305: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- controls
 306: A = Addr(CODE)
 307: t, alive = session(store(A, SCRATCH, LIVE_VAL) + load(A, SCRATCH) + ["print v0"])
 308: row("live", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", "0x%x" % LIVE_VAL,
 309:     "LIVENESS: guest store then load, through RAM")
 310: 
 311: A = Addr(CODE)
 312: t, alive = session(load(A, DE_STATUS) + ["print v0"])
 313: row("devsig", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", DE_STAT,
 314:     "DEVSIG: CRIME_DE_STATUS reads 0x1e000000; RAM reads 0")
 315: 
 316: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the accept control
 317: #  Both modes LIN_A: the copy is the operation the defect corrupts.  If this row does not
 318: #  land the source value, the probe has not driven the engine and the defect row below is
 319: #  VACUOUS -- which is why it is a row and not an assumption.
 320: A = Addr(CODE)
 321: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_LIN_A) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 322: row("copy lin_a", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", "0x%x" % SRC_VAL,
 323:     "ACCEPT: LIN_A source copies to the destination")
 324: 
 325: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the defect
 326: #  Source RGB, destination RGBA.  The XFER GET decodes the source with the RGB arm and the PUT
 327: #  encodes it with the RGBA arm, so the destination shows exactly what the RGB GET produced.
 328: #
 329: #  *** THESE ROWS ARE WRITTEN RED-NOW / GREEN-AFTER, WHICH IS THE ONLY SHAPE A WITNESS MAY SHIP IN.
 330: #  An earlier draft had them the other way round -- asserting the CORRUPT value, so both would have
 331: #  gone RED the moment the fix landed.  That is a fine one-shot demonstration and an unshippable
 332: #  gate row, and the ladder says so in clause (i): a witness shows the symptom on the pre-fix
 333: #  committed HEAD AND GOES GREEN POST-FIX WITH THE SAME SCRIPT.  The exact pre-fix value is not
 334: #  lost by the inversion -- it is pinned by `rgb not corrupt' below and printed in the GOT column
 335: #  of every run, so the pre-fix log records it verbatim without any row having to expect it. ***
 336: A = Addr(CODE)
 337: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 338: got_rgb = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 339: 
 340: #  THE FIX'S SUCCESS CRITERION.  RED on the committed build (it reads 0xbbccdd00), GREEN once
 341: #  GET RGB reads buf[0..2].  It pins the EXACT post-fix value rather than merely "not the corrupt
 342: #  one", so a fix that moves the bytes to some THIRD wrong place still reds.
 343: row("rgb chan", got_rgb, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 344:     "R,G,B must survive at their own byte offsets; the pad is not carried")
 345: 
 346: #  FLOOR CLAUSE (b): reds if the removed defect is reinstated VERBATIM.  It is not redundant with
 347: #  the row above -- that one fails for ANY wrong value, this one names the SPECIFIC wrong value, so
 348: #  a future edit that reintroduces the off-by-one is distinguishable from one that breaks the copy
 349: #  some other way.  Both are needed and neither implies the other.
 350: row("rgb not corrupt", got_rgb, "0x%x" % RGB_CORRUPT,
 351:     "must not be the buf[1..3] shift: 0xaabbccdd -> 0xbbccdd00", negate=True)
 352: 
 353: row("rgb alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive",
 354:     "the host survives: this is a wrong-value defect, not a kill")
 355: 
 356: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- same-mode RGB -> RGB
 357: #  *** ASKED FOR INDEPENDENTLY BY TWO PASS-1 SEATS, and the reason is coverage this file did not
 358: #  have: every row above is an RGB->RGBA copy, so the RGB *PUT* arm is never executed. ***
 359: #      grok: "pin both with a same-mode RT row AND a raw-memory GET whose byte 3 is nonzero"
 360: #      glm:  "a same-mode RGB->RGB copy, raw-seeded -- the most guest-realistic corruption
 361: #             (today every pixel word shifts left 8) and the natural detector row; S4 is
 362: #             RGB->RGBA only"
 363: #
 364: #  It is also the shape a real guest takes, which the mixed-mode row is not: a driver that has
 365: #  programmed RGB does not usually change the destination type mid-copy.
 366: #
 367: #  THE VALUES ARE THE SAME AS THE MIXED-MODE ROW, AND THAT IS NOT REDUNDANCY.  PUT RGB writes the
 368: #  top three bytes of whatever GET produced and zeroes buf[3], so the destination word matches the
 369: #  RGBA case -- but the code path executed is different, so a mutant in the RGB PUT arm reds HERE
 370: #  and is invisible to every row above.
 371: #
 372: #  grok's "byte 3 nonzero" condition is met by SRC_VAL itself: 0xaabbccdd has 0xdd at byte 3, so
 373: #  this row additionally proves the pad is NOT carried rather than merely being zero already.
 374: A = Addr(CODE)
 375: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB, dst_mode=MODE_RGB) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 376: got_same = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 377: 
 378: row("rgb samemode", got_same, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 379:     "RGB->RGB: exercises the RGB PUT arm, which no other row reaches")
 380: 
 381: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the WIDTH axis
 382: #  *** THIS ROW CLOSES A MEASURED CLAUSE-(d) FLOOR BREACH.  L2, repaired in-round. ***
 383: #
 384: #  Table 1 built the canonical clause-(d) mutant -- guard KEPT, repair CONDITIONED on a field the
 385: #  guarded body reads:
 386: #
 387: #      case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 388: #              if (bufdepth == 4)
 389: #                      *color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8);   /* the fix */
 390: #              else
 391: #                      *color = (buf[1] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[3] << 8);   /* the defect */
 392: #
 393: #  and it scored a CLEAN SWEEP, 6/6, against every row above -- because EVERY session in this file
 394: #  used BUFDEPTH_32, so bufdepth is 4 in all of them and the mutant's fix branch is the only one the
 395: #  detector ever executes.  **The detector proved the fix works exactly where it was measured and
 396: #  said nothing about anywhere else.**
 397: #
 398: #  `bufdepth' is squarely inside the DEPENDS-ON closure: it is read by the guarded body (it is
 399: #  memory_rw's transfer length) and it comes from bits 8-9 of the SAME mode word whose bits 4-7 the
 400: #  guard reads.  So this is not an exotic mutant -- it is the shape the closure enumerates first.
 401: #
 402: #  Driving a SECOND width is what makes the width axis observable at all.  bufdepth 8 keeps
 403: #  buf[0..2] filled by memory_rw, so the expected value is unchanged and the row stays a value
 404: #  oracle; bufdepth 1 or 2 would leave buf[1..2] uninitialised, which is `sgirebufuninit' and would
 405: #  make this row non-deterministic -- so 8 is the only other width that can carry a value oracle.
 406: A = Addr(CODE)
 407: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB64, stepx=8) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 408: got_d64 = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 409: 
 410: row("rgb depth64", got_d64, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 411:     "the fix must hold at bufdepth 8, not only at the width the other rows use")
 412: 
 413: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the ADDRESSING-MODE axis
 414: #  *** THIS ROW CLOSES A SECOND MEASURED CLAUSE-(d) FLOOR BREACH, found by pass 2. ***
 415: #
 416: #  Mutant M12_linear -- guard kept, repair conditioned on `linear':
 417: #
 418: #      case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 419: #              if (linear) *color = (buf[0]<<24)+(buf[1]<<16)+(buf[2]<<8);   /* the fix    */
 420: #              else        *color = (buf[1]<<24)+(buf[2]<<16)+(buf[3]<<8);   /* the defect */
 421: #
 422: #  scored **8/8, a CLEAN SWEEP**, because every row above drives LIN_A (tlb_mode 4) and `linear' is
 423: #  therefore true in all of them.  `linear' is `tlb_mode > 3' and it DOMINATES the edited lines via
 424: #  the `if (!linear && (x < 0 || ...)) return;' guard, so it sits inside the DEPENDS-ON closure --
 425: #  this is a floor breach, not coverage debt.
 426: #
 427: #  The seat that named the axis proposed conditioning on `tileptr'.  That cannot work as stated:
 428: #  `tileptr &= ~0x80000000' strips the linear flag BEFORE the switch, so `tileptr' carries no
 429: #  addressing-mode information by the time the arm runs.  `linear' is the variable that survives, and
 430: #  building the mutant in the form the source permits is what turned a prediction into a measurement.
 431: #
 432: #  THE SOURCE is tiled and the DESTINATION stays LIN_A, deliberately: the RGB arm under test is the
 433: #  GET, and the GET's `linear' comes from src_mode.  A tiled DESTINATION would exercise the PUT and
 434: #  leave this mutant untouched.
 435: A = Addr(CODE)
 436: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB_TLBA, tiled_src=True) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 437: got_tiled = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 438: 
 439: row("rgb tiled", got_tiled, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 440:     "the fix must hold for a TILED source, not only for LIN_A")
 441: 
 442: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the JOINT cell
 443: #  *** THIS ROW CLOSES THE CROSS-PRODUCT, AND IT EXISTS BECAUSE I REPAIRED CELL-BY-CELL TWICE. ***
 444: #
 445: #  Two axes were each closed on their own:  WIDTH by `rgb depth64' (LIN_A, bufdepth 8) and
 446: #  ADDRESSING by `rgb tiled' (tiled, bufdepth 4).  That leaves a 2x2 with one corner empty:
 447: #
 448: #                     bufdepth 4                  bufdepth 8
 449: #      LIN_A          rgb chan / rgb samemode     rgb depth64
 450: #      tiled          rgb tiled                   *** was UNROWED ***
 451: #
 452: #  and a repair conditioned on `(bufdepth == 4 || linear)' is TRUE in all three rowed cells, so it
 453: #  scores 9/9 BY CONSTRUCTION.  Three pass-3 seats named that cell independently -- grok, the kimi
 454: #  substitute and deepseek -- and the kimi seat wrote the mutant out in exactly that form.
 455: #
 456: #  The carrier's class-closure amendment is explicit about the remedy: when two consecutive passes
 457: #  find breaches of ONE CLASS, the next repair owes the CLASS CLOSURE -- enumerate the full space and
 458: #  close every cell in ONE diff, rather than adding the cell the latest mutant happened to name.
 459: #  **Repairing at exactly the address the mutant used is what produced breaches two and three.**
 460: #
 461: #  Geometry check for bufdepth 8 under tiled addressing: tilewidth_in_pixels = 512/8 = 64, so
 462: #  tile_nr = (y>>7)*16 + x/64 = 0 for x=0, y=0..3, and ofs = 512*y + (x % 64)*8 = 512*y -- the same
 463: #  four source rows TLBA_ROWS already paints.  No new setup is needed, only the mode and the step.
 464: A = Addr(CODE)
 465: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB_TLBA64, tiled_src=True, stepx=8)
 466:                    + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 467: got_t64 = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 468: 
 469: row("rgb tiled64", got_t64, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 470:     "the JOINT cell: tiled source AND bufdepth 8, which no other row reaches")
 471: 
 472: ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
 473: print("")
 474: print("SGIRGB_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
 475: print("SGIRGB_WITNESS=%s" % ("PASS" if ok == len(rows) else "FAIL"))
 476: print("SGIRGB_DONE")
 477: sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: #
   3: #  lcaconfwidth -- RUNG-3 WITNESS: the LCA IOC_CONF kill diagnostic names a value the guard ACCEPTS.
   4: #
   5: #  THE DEFECT, dev_lca.c, `case LCA_IOC_CONF:'
   6: #
   7: #      d->ioc_conf = idata;                     /* the FULL 64-bit value is stored   */
   8: #      if (idata & ~1) {                        /* the guard sees all 64 bits        */
   9: #              fatal("TODO: Write to unimplemented bit of"
  10: #                  " IOC_CONF: 0x%x\n", (int)idata);   /* the message sees 32        */
  11: #              exit(1);
  12: #      }
  13: #
  14: #  A guest `stq' of 0x100000000 sets bit 32.  `idata & ~1' is true, so the process dies -- and
  15: #  `(int)idata' is 0, which is a value this guard ACCEPTS.  The kill message names an acceptable
  16: #  value while killing on an unacceptable one.  That is the whole defect, and it is observable.
  17: #
  18: #  *** WHAT THIS PROBE IS AND IS NOT.  It is a WITNESS (rung 3): it shows the symptom on the
  19: #  COMMITTED build, which is what licenses a fix.  It is not a detector and must not be gated --
  20: #  a witness stays red until the fix lands. ***
  21: #
  22: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  23: #  FOUR THINGS THE DESIGN REVIEW (grok, 2026-09-07) KILLED BEFORE THIS WAS WRITTEN.  Each would
  24: #  have produced a green or an unreadable run, and none is obvious from the source alone:
  25: #
  26: #  1. `load_const' CANNOT BUILD EITHER OPERAND.  LDA/LDAH make 32-bit sign-extended constants.
  27: #     The K0SEG address needs bits 63..42 and the test value needs bit 32.  So both 64-bit words
  28: #     are planted in RAM with debugger `put d' and loaded with `ldq' while `ptbr' is still 0 --
  29: #     the same bootstrap alpha_harness_probe.py uses, and the only one that works.
  30: #
  31: #  2. DEBUGGER `dump' CANNOT BE THE READ-BACK.  `dump' calls memory_rw with NO_EXCEPTIONS, and
  32: #     LCA registers DM_DEFAULT rather than DM_READS_HAVE_NO_SIDE_EFFECTS, so the handler is
  33: #     SKIPPED and the caller gets the pre-zeroed buffer.  That is the UNMAPPED SIGNATURE PRODUCED
  34: #     BY A HIT -- a false negative that looks exactly like a miss.  The read-back is a guest `ldq'.
  35: #
  36: #  3. `put d' OF THE KILLING VALUE IS NOT RUNG-3.  store_64bit_word goes through memory_rw with
  37: #     CACHE_DATA and no NO_EXCEPTIONS, so it DOES enter the handler and exit(1)s -- a real kill,
  38: #     caused by no guest instruction.  The killing store must be a guest `stq'.
  39: #
  40: #  4. A MISS IS NOT ONLY "UNMAPPED -> NOTHING".  Offset 0 of the window is LCA_IOC_BASE (HAE),
  41: #     which `break's with no kill.  A store to k0(0x180000000) is SILENT WHILE INSIDE the LCA
  42: #     window.  Unmapped and HAE both read back 0, so the control has to tell them apart -- which
  43: #     is what `ioc rt' does: only the real IOC_CONF arm stores AND returns the value.
  44: #
  45: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  46: #  HONEST LIMIT, stated because the row must not claim a language guarantee: `(int)0x100000000 == 0'
  47: #  is IMPLEMENTATION-DEFINED, not C99.  GCC and Clang truncate to the low 32 bits, which is what
  48: #  this tree is built with, so the witness holds on this build and is not a statement about C.
  49: #
  50: #  Usage:  lca_confwidth_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
  51: import os, sys, pty, select, time, re, struct
  52: 
  53: BIN   = sys.argv[1]
  54: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
  55: MACH  = "alphabook1"          #  device_add(machine, "lca") is ST_ALPHABOOK1 ONLY
  56: 
  57: rows = []
  58: def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
  59:     ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
  60:     rows.append((name, ok))
  61:     shown = ("NOT " + str(want)) if negate else str(want)
  62:     print("%-16s %-22s %-24s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), shown, note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
  63:     return ok
  64: 
  65: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- encoder (memory format only)
  66: ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4
  67: 
  68: def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
  69:     assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
  70:     return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)
  71: 
  72: def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
  73: def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
  74: def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
  75: def STQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x2d, ra, rb, disp)
  76: 
  77: def load_const(reg, val):
  78:     #  32-bit only, and deliberately so: this builds the SCRATCH pointer, never a 64-bit operand.
  79:     lo = val & 0xffff
  80:     hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
  81:     if lo & 0x8000:                      # LDA sign-extends its displacement
  82:         hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
  83:     ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
  84:     ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
  85:     return ops
  86: 
  87: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- addresses (ALL RAM < 64 MB)
  88: K0SEG       = 0xfffffc0000000000
  89: def k0(p):  return K0SEG | p
  90: 
  91: LCA_IOC_BASE = 0x180000000
  92: LCA_IOC_CONF = LCA_IOC_BASE + 0x020          # alpha_lcareg.h
  93: SCRATCH      = 0x01020000                    # the three 64-bit words live here
  94: CODE         = 0x01040000
  95: LEGAL        = 0x1                           # 1 & ~1 == 0 -> accepted, no kill
  96: KILLER       = 0x100000000                   # bit 32 -> guard fires; (int) of it is 0
  97: 
  98: #  The same guard the Alpha harness needed: an address past physical_max is written to nothing and
  99: #  read back as zero, SILENTLY.  -M 64 gives 64 MB.
 100: assert max(SCRATCH, CODE) < 64 * 1024 * 1024, "scratch/code must be inside RAM"
 101: 
 102: DUMMY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")
 103: if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
 104:     with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
 105:         f.write(b"\0" * 8)
 106: 
 107: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- session
 108: def session(cmds, settle=1.6, spin=2.2):
 109:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 110:     if pid == 0:
 111:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
 112:                        "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
 113:         os._exit(127)
 114:     buf = ""
 115:     def rd(t):
 116:         nonlocal buf
 117:         end = time.time() + t
 118:         while time.time() < end:
 119:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 120:             if not r:
 121:                 continue
 122:             try:
 123:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 124:                 if not d:
 125:                     return False
 126:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 127:             except OSError:
 128:                 return False
 129:         return True
 130:     alive = rd(settle)
 131:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 132:         try:
 133:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 134:         except OSError:
 135:             alive = False; break
 136:         if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.3):
 137:             alive = False; break
 138:     try:
 139:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
 140:     except OSError:
 141:         pass
 142:     try:
 143:         os.close(fd)
 144:     except OSError:
 145:         pass
 146:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive
 147: 
 148: def printed(t, reg):
 149:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 150:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)
 151: 
 152: print("lca_confwidth_probe -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH))
 153: print("%-16s %-22s %-24s %-46s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))
 154: 
 155: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- SESSION 1: liveness + control
 156: #  Everything up to and including the legal round-trip, WITHOUT the killing store, so these rows
 157: #  are readable on a build where the killing store would end the process.
 158: setup = ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 0,  k0(LCA_IOC_CONF)),
 159:          "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 8,  LEGAL),
 160:          "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 16, KILLER)]
 161: 
 162: prog_ctl = (load_const(T0, SCRATCH) +
 163:             [LDQ(T1, 0, T0),        # t1 = k0(LCA_IOC_CONF)
 164:              LDQ(T2, 8, T0),        # t2 = 0x1
 165:              STQ(T2, 0, T1),        # LEGAL store into the device
 166:              LDQ(T3, 0, T1)])       # guest read-back through the SAME decode
 167: cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_ctl)]
 168: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_ctl), "print t3"]
 169: t1txt, alive1 = session(cmds)
 170: 
 171: row("live", "alive" if alive1 else "dead", "alive",
 172:     "the control session survives the legal write")
 173: row("ioc rt", printed(t1txt, "t3"), "0x1",
 174:     "guest stq 0x1 then guest ldq through the SAME decode")
 175: 
 176: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- SESSION 2: the killing store
 177: #  The killing store is LAST because nothing runs after it.
 178: prog_kill = (load_const(T0, SCRATCH) +
 179:              [LDQ(T1, 0, T0),       # t1 = k0(LCA_IOC_CONF)
 180:               LDQ(T2, 16, T0),      # t2 = 0x100000000
 181:               STQ(T2, 0, T1)])      # THE KILLING STORE
 182: cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_kill)]
 183: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_kill)]
 184: t2txt, alive2 = session(cmds)
 185: 
 186: #  *** PARSE THE VALUE; DO NOT SUBSTRING-MATCH IT. ***
 187: #  These rows used `"IOC_CONF: 0x100000000" in text'.  A message of 0x1000000000 -- one extra
 188: #  literal zero, a plausible edit at this very site -- CONTAINS that string, so the row went
 189: #  GREEN on a wrong diagnostic, and the displayed `got' was manufactured from the boolean rather
 190: #  than read from the output.  `zero' had the mirror flaw (0x0abc contains 0x0).
 191: #  Found by a pass-2 review of this round, not by the round itself.
 192: m_ioc = re.search(r"IOC_CONF: (0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)", t2txt)
 193: hit   = m_ioc is not None
 194: ioc   = int(m_ioc.group(1), 16) if m_ioc else None
 195: shown = ("0x%x" % ioc) if m_ioc else "absent"
 196: zero  = (ioc == 0)
 197: full  = (ioc == KILLER)
 198: 
 199: #  *** ORACLE POLARITY.  The ladder requires a witness to show the symptom on the PRE-FIX build and
 200: #  go GREEN post-fix WITH THE SAME SCRIPT.  So these rows assert the CORRECT behaviour and are
 201: #  expected to FAIL here.  The first draft of this probe asserted the DEFECT as the passing state
 202: #  and scored 6/6 on the committed build -- which reads as success and would have gone RED the
 203: #  moment the bug was fixed.  That is a detector-of-the-defect wearing a witness's clothes, and it
 204: #  is the opposite of what clause (i) asks for. ***
 205: row("kill fired", "yes" if hit else "no", "yes",
 206:     "CONTROL: the IOC_CONF guard announced -- not HAE, not unmapped, not a skipped handler")
 207: row("kill width", shown, "0x%x" % KILLER,
 208:     "the message must name the value that ACTUALLY killed")
 209: #  *** AN INEQUALITY PASSES ON EVERY NON-OBSERVATION.  Repaired 2026-09-07 -- see the same note
 210: #  in alpha_harness_probe.py.  With the diagnostic REMOVED but the terminating call kept, `shown'
 211: #  is "absent", which is != "0x0" and scored GREEN.  `kill fired' reds on that, so the detector
 212: #  did not escape; this row's note claimed a control it did not have. ***
 213: notzero = hit and ioc != 0
 214: row("kill notzero", "yes" if notzero else shown, "yes",
 215:     "the diagnostic must NAME a nonzero value -- 0x0 is a value this guard ACCEPTS, and 'absent' is no diagnostic at all")
 216: row("died", "died" if not alive2 else "alive", "died",
 217:     "CONTROL: exit(1) really ran -- distinguishes a kill from a silent decline")
 218: 
 219: ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
 220: print()
 221: print("LCACONF_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
 222: print("LCACONF_DONE")
 223: sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)
GXEMUL-SEC/regress/alpha_pfnoffset_probe.py
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: #
   3: #  alphapfnoffset -- RUNG-3 WITNESS: the Alpha page-table walk drops the within-page offset.
   4: #
   5: #      memory_alpha.c   (*return_paddr) = ALPHA_PTE_TO_PFN(pte3) << pageSizeShift;
   6: #      memory_rw.c      offset = paddr & offset_mask;
   7: #
   8: #  The walk returns a PAGE BASE, so `paddr & offset_mask' is zero and a walked access resolves to
   9: #  the page's first bytes.  A codex reachability pass enumerated all 8192 offsets: the walk yields
  10: #  offset zero throughout, differing from the offset-preserving address for 8191 of them.
  11: #
  12: #  *** THIS IS A WITNESS (rung 3), NOT A DETECTOR.  It shows the symptom on the COMMITTED build,
  13: #  which is what licenses a fix, and it must NOT be gated -- a witness stays red until the fix
  14: #  lands.  Its rows therefore assert the CORRECT behaviour and are expected to FAIL here. ***
  15: #
  16: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  17: #  SIX THINGS THE DESIGN REVIEW (grok, 2026-09-07) ESTABLISHED.  Four are traps that would have
  18: #  produced a GREEN run on the defective build; none is obvious from the source.
  19: #
  20: #  1. THE SWAP IS NOT A BLOCKER.  `swpctx' is the only writer of apcb_ptbr -- but it is only a
  21: #     CONFOUND when ptbr and flags DIFFER.  Put the SAME L1 PFN in both PCB fields and the
  22: #     defective line and the corrected line both leave apcb_ptbr = L1_PFN.  (The inverse is a
  23: #     trap: PFN in flags with ptbr = 0 arms the walk on HEAD and leaves IDENTITY MAPPING on a
  24: #     fixed PAL, so post-fix green would be the bootstrap, not the repair.)
  25: #
  26: #  2. PFN, NOT BYTE ADDRESS.  apcb_ptbr and the PTE high halves hold PFNs.  ptbr = 0x01050000
  27: #     would give base = 0x01050000 << 13, past RAM, and the walk reports `wasn't found'.
  28: #
  29: #  3. ONE COLD SHOT.  The fast path caches the page from `paddr & ~offset_mask' -- the CORRECT
  30: #     physical page -- so the walked paddr is wrong only for the FILL.  A second access at any
  31: #     offset is a hit and reads the right bytes, i.e. GOES GREEN ON HEAD.  Load once; never retry.
  32: #
  33: #  4. THE POLARITY TRAP.  Planting B *through the walk* makes HEAD store it at data+0 and read it
  34: #     back from data+0 -- the defect measured against itself, green.  A cousin: planting at vaddr
  35: #     0/N while ptbr == 0 hits PHYSICAL 0/N, not the data page.  So A and B are planted through
  36: #     K0SEG of the DATA PAGE, before the walk is ever used.
  37: #
  38: #  5. A FAILED WALK DOES NOT WRITE THE DESTINATION REGISTER.  LS_GENERIC_N returns on a memory_rw
  39: #     failure without storing, so "faults or returns zero" is false.  Hence a THREE-WAY oracle
  40: #     with a sentinel S pre-loaded into the destination:
  41: #         S = the walk never completed        0 = walked onto unmapped physical
  42: #         A = right page, offset dropped      B = correct
  43: #
  44: #  6. ONLY ALPHA_PTE_VALID IS LOAD-BEARING.  Verified in memory_alpha.c: each level tests
  45: #     `!(pteN & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)' and nothing else -- not KR/KW/UR/UW, FAULT_ON_*, ASM or
  46: #     granularity.  Setting bits the walk does not implement and calling them required would be
  47: #     the "cannot hurt" addition this project refuses.
  48: #
  49: #  Usage:  alpha_pfnoffset_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
  50: import os, sys, pty, select, time, re
  51: 
  52: BIN   = sys.argv[1]
  53: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
  54: MACH  = "alphabook1"
  55: 
  56: rows = []
  57: def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
  58:     ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
  59:     rows.append((name, ok))
  60:     shown = ("NOT " + str(want)) if negate else str(want)
  61:     print("%-14s %-20s %-22s %-44s %s" % (name, str(got), shown, note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
  62:     return ok
  63: 
  64: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- encoder
  65: ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
  66: A0 = 16
  67: 
  68: def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
  69:     assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
  70:     return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)
  71: 
  72: def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
  73: def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
  74: def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
  75: def CALL_PAL(n):             return n & 0x3ffffff
  76: 
  77: def load_const(reg, val):
  78:     lo, hi = val & 0xffff, (val >> 16) & 0xffff
  79:     if lo & 0x8000:
  80:         hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
  81:     return [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000),
  82:             LDA (reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg)]
  83: 
  84: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the map
  85: K0SEG      = 0xfffffc0000000000
  86: def k0(p): return K0SEG | p
  87: 
  88: PAGESHIFT  = 13
  89: PTE_VALID  = 0x1
  90: def pte(pfn): return (pfn << 32) | PTE_VALID
  91: 
  92: #  Off the PROM pocket (0x10000 entry stub, 0x10100 initial ctx, 0x12000 argspace, 0x14000 HWRPB)
  93: #  and off the sibling probes' 0x0102/0103/0104xxxx scratch.
  94: P_L1, P_L2, P_L3, P_DATA = 0x01050000, 0x01052000, 0x01054000, 0x01056000
  95: P_PCB, P_CODE            = 0x01058000, 0x0105a000
  96: P_DATA2                  = 0x0105c000          # mapped at vaddr 0x2000 via L3[1]
  97: L1_PFN = P_L1 >> PAGESHIFT
  98: 
  99: #  -M 64.  A page past physical_max is written to nothing and read back as zero, SILENTLY.
 100: assert max(P_L1, P_L2, P_L3, P_DATA, P_DATA2, P_PCB, P_CODE) < 64 * 1024 * 1024
 101: assert all(p % 8192 == 0 for p in (P_L1, P_L2, P_L3, P_DATA, P_DATA2)), "pages must be page-aligned"
 102: 
 103: VADDR = 0x0000            # i1 = i2 = i3 = 0
 104: N     = 8                 # aligned: an unaligned N forces the generic path for the WRONG reason
 105: VAL_A = 0xaaaa0000aaaa    # planted at data + 0   -- what the DEFECT returns
 106: VAL_B = 0xbbbb0000bbbb    # planted at data + N   -- what a CORRECT walk returns
 107: #  ---- the SECOND cold row: non-zero VPN, offset in the UPPER 4 KB ----
 108: VADDR2 = 0x2000           # i1 = i2 = 0, i3 = 1  -- the VPN is NON-ZERO, which is the point
 109: N2     = 0x1008           # upper 4 KB, 8-aligned
 110: VAL_A2 = 0xcccc0000cccc   # data2 + 0        <- what a page-base result reads
 111: VAL_B2 = 0xdddd0000dddd   # data2 + 0x1008   <- CORRECT
 112: VAL_C2 = 0xeeee0000eeee   # data2 + 0x0008   <- what a 0xfff mask, or `| 8', reads
 113: assert (VADDR2 >> 13) == 1, "VADDR2 must select L3[1] -- a NON-ZERO VPN is the whole point"
 114: assert N2 >= 0x1000 and N2 % 8 == 0, "N2 must sit in the upper 4 KB and be 8-aligned"
 115: 
 116: VAL_S = 0x55000055        # sentinel: unchanged means the load never stored.  32-bit with
 117:                           # bit 31 CLEAR, because load_const builds it -- a wider value
 118:                           # would silently truncate and the row would compare the wrong thing.
 119: 
 120: DUMMY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")
 121: if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
 122:     with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
 123:         f.write(b"\0" * 8)
 124: 
 125: def session(cmds, settle=1.6, spin=2.4):
 126:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 127:     if pid == 0:
 128:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
 129:                        "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
 130:         os._exit(127)
 131:     buf = ""
 132:     def rd(t):
 133:         nonlocal buf
 134:         end = time.time() + t
 135:         while time.time() < end:
 136:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 137:             if not r:
 138:                 continue
 139:             try:
 140:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 141:                 if not d:
 142:                     return False
 143:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 144:             except OSError:
 145:                 return False
 146:         return True
 147:     alive = rd(settle)
 148:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 149:         try:
 150:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 151:         except OSError:
 152:             alive = False; break
 153:         if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.3):
 154:             alive = False; break
 155:     try:
 156:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
 157:     except OSError:
 158:         pass
 159:     try:
 160:         os.close(fd)
 161:     except OSError:
 162:         pass
 163:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive
 164: 
 165: def printed(t, reg):
 166:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 167:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)
 168: 
 169: print("alpha_pfnoffset_probe -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH))
 170: print("%-14s %-20s %-22s %-44s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))
 171: 
 172: #  ---- setup: tables, data and the PCB, ALL planted while ptbr is still 0 ----------------------
 173: #  K0SEG for the data page (trap 4); the table pages are equally reachable either way.
 174: setup = [
 175:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L1),        pte(P_L2 >> PAGESHIFT)),
 176:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L2),        pte(P_L3 >> PAGESHIFT)),
 177:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L3),        pte(P_DATA >> PAGESHIFT)),
 178:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA + 0),  VAL_A),
 179:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA + N),  VAL_B),
 180:     #  the second page, and L3[1] -> it.  Entry 1 is at byte offset 8 of the L3 page.
 181:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L3 + 8),    pte(P_DATA2 >> PAGESHIFT)),
 182:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA2 + 0),      VAL_A2),
 183:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA2 + 8),      VAL_C2),
 184:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA2 + N2),     VAL_B2),
 185:     #  PCB: ptbr AND flags both the L1 PFN, so the swpctx defect cannot change what gets armed.
 186:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_PCB + 16),  L1_PFN),     # apcb_ptbr
 187:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_PCB + 40),  L1_PFN),     # apcb_flags
 188:     "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_CODE - 8),  k0(P_PCB)),  # a0 source for swpctx
 189: ]
 190: 
 191: #  ---- SESSION 1: the K0SEG controls, before any walk ------------------------------------------
 192: #  T0 must hold a K0SEG address, which load_const CANNOT build (it makes 32-bit sign-extended
 193: #  constants and K0SEG needs bits 63..42).  So the pointer is planted in memory and read with ldq,
 194: #  which is the same bootstrap the two sibling Alpha probes use.
 195: cmds = setup + ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_CODE - 16), k0(P_DATA))]
 196: prog_ctl = (load_const(T3, P_CODE - 16) +
 197:             [LDQ(T0, 0, T3),      # t0 = k0(P_DATA)
 198:              LDQ(T1, 0, T0),      # t1 = *(data + 0)   -- must be A
 199:              LDQ(T2, N, T0)])     # t2 = *(data + N)   -- must be B
 200: cmds += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_ctl)]
 201: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_ctl), "print t1", "print t2"]
 202: t1txt, alive1 = session(cmds)
 203: 
 204: row("data0 k0", printed(t1txt, "t1"), "0x%x" % VAL_A,
 205:     "CONTROL: A really is at data+0")
 206: row("dataN k0", printed(t1txt, "t2"), "0x%x" % VAL_B,
 207:     "CONTROL: B really is at data+N")
 208: 
 209: #  ---- SESSION 2: arm the walk, then ONE cold walked load --------------------------------------
 210: prog_walk = (load_const(T3, P_CODE - 8) +
 211:              [LDQ(A0, 0, T3),               # a0 = k0(P_PCB)
 212:               CALL_PAL(0x30)] +             # swpctx -> apcb_ptbr = L1_PFN
 213:              load_const(T4, VAL_S) +
 214:              [LDQ(T4, VADDR + N, ZERO)])    # THE ONE COLD WALKED LOAD
 215: cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_walk)]
 216: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_walk), "print t4"]
 217: t2txt, alive2 = session(cmds)
 218: 
 219: dest     = printed(t2txt, "t4")
 220: notfound = "wasn't found" in t2txt
 221: 
 222: #  ---- SESSION 3: the second cold load, non-zero VPN, offset in the upper 4 KB ---------------
 223: #  A SEPARATE session because the load must be COLD: the fast path caches the correct physical
 224: #  page, so a second access in the same run is a hit and reads the right bytes on HEAD.
 225: prog_walk2 = (load_const(T3, P_CODE - 8) +
 226:               [LDQ(A0, 0, T3), CALL_PAL(0x30)] +
 227:               load_const(T4, VAL_S) +
 228:               [LDQ(T4, VADDR2 + N2, ZERO)])
 229: cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_walk2)]
 230: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_walk2), "print t4"]
 231: t3txt, alive3 = session(cmds)
 232: dest2 = printed(t3txt, "t4")
 233: 
 234: row("walk found", "absent" if not notfound else "wasn't found", "absent",
 235:     "CONTROL: the table walked to a valid L3")
 236: #  *** AN INEQUALITY PASSES ON EVERY NON-OBSERVATION.  Repaired 2026-09-07 -- see the same note
 237: #  in alpha_harness_probe.py.  Both rows below scored GREEN when `dest' was "absent" or "died",
 238: #  i.e. when the readback never happened.  `walk off' reds on those, so the detector did not
 239: #  escape; the defect was in these two rows' claim to be CONTROLS. ***
 240: obs = dest.startswith("0x") and dest != "0x0"
 241: stored = obs and dest != ("0x%x" % VAL_S)
 242: row("walk stored", "yes" if stored else dest, "yes",
 243:     "CONTROL: an OBSERVED word that is not the sentinel -- the sentinel surviving, or no readback at all, means the load never wrote t4")
 244: row("walk mapped", "yes" if obs else dest, "yes",
 245:     "CONTROL: a real nonzero word -- 0x0/absent/died all mean the walk found no physical page")
 246: row("walk off", dest, "0x%x" % VAL_B,
 247:     "the walked load must return data+N, not data+0")
 248: 
 249: #  *** THE SECOND COLD ROW.  Six lesser edits score 6/6 on the row above; three of them die
 250: #  here, and the three-way plant says WHICH: ***
 251: #      VAL_B2  correct
 252: #      VAL_C2  a 0xfff mask (the 4 KB copy) or a hardcoded `| 8'
 253: #      VAL_A2  a page-base result
 254: #  0x3fff and 0x7fff are EQUIVALENT inside an 8 KB page -- bits 13/14 of an offset are always
 255: #  zero there -- so no row kills them and none should pretend to.
 256: row("walk vpn", dest2, "0x%x" % VAL_B2,
 257:     "non-zero VPN, offset in the UPPER 4 KB: kills the 0xfff mask and unmasked +vaddr")

I found a separate `swpctx` mismatch: its comment says the old context is returned in `v0`, but the code writes `a0`. I’m checking the witness predicates and the remaining comment claims.

--
150- *  It is ONE BOUNDARY IN 1024, not 256: MRW_SPLIT_MASK is 0x3ff, so the split granule is
151- *  1024 bytes and 1 MB / 1024 = 1024.  The old "256" came from dividing 1 MB by a 4 KB page
152- *  -- a granule this split does not use, as the note above says: "The granule the SPLIT
153- *  needs is a different one."  (And per that note the real page here is 8 KB anyway.)
154- *
155- *  Two properties of the memblock split, both MEASURED:
156: *    - post-#430 it fires only on PHYSICAL accesses.  A TRANSLATED 4096-byte write across a
157- *      1 MB boundary is chunked at the 1024-byte SPLIT granule, so it costs 4 translations
158- *      and 4 host lookups and never reaches here; PHYSICAL costs 0 and 2.  A chunk of at
159- *      most one granule, granule-aligned, cannot cross 1 MB.
160- *    - its head can NEVER be a device, in either version: device dispatch `goto
161- *      do_return_ok`s first.  Measured with a device straddling the memblock boundary --
162- *      one device call, offset 0xffe, len 4, zero host lookups.
--
172- *  loop is still the right implementation (it does not depend on an optimisation being
173- *  applied, and -O0 builds and other compilers are real), but the justification was wrong,
174- *  and the row that certified it was VACUOUS until the differential was given
175- *  -fno-optimize-sibling-calls.  No caller in the tree passes an access large enough for
176- *  this to bite today; it is insurance, and it costs about fifteen lines.
177- *
178: *  ONLY WHEN A TRANSLATION ACTUALLY HAPPENS.  Under PHYSICAL (or with no translate_v2p)
179- *  paddr == vaddr, there is no per-page mapping to redo, and splitting would buy nothing
180- *  while turning one large DMA into thousands of calls -- so those go straight through and

 334: DEVINIT(lca)
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: #  alpha_harness_probe.py -- THE ALPHA RUNG-3 HARNESS.
   3: #
   4: #  There has never been an Alpha probe in this tree, and three open HIGH rows need one
   5: #  (`alphaswpctxptbr', `alphapfnoffset', and the swpctx return-register defect).  This file is
   6: #  the FOUNDATION: an encoder, a pty session, and the two controls the witness ladder demands
   7: #  of any rung-3 access witness.  It deliberately ships the controls FIRST and asserts nothing
   8: #  about any defect, because a harness whose controls have never been green cannot be trusted
   9: #  to say anything about a defect afterwards.
  10: #
  11: #  *** WHAT IS ESTABLISHED BEFORE THIS FILE RUNS, all read out of the fork rather than assumed:
  12: #
  13: #  1. THE MACHINES EXIST AND THREE OF FOUR RUN.  Measured 2026-09-07: `-H' advertises
  14: #     alphabook1 (21066), alphaserver4100 (21164A-2), 3000/300 and eb164 (21164PC); the first,
  15: #     second and fourth construct and reach the debugger prompt, and `3000/300' ABORTS the host
  16: #     during construction at interrupt_handler_lookup("0") -- a separate filed defect.
  17: #
  18: #  2. `call_pal' IS OPCODE 0 WITH THE PAL NUMBER IN BITS 25..0, and cpu_alpha_instr.c dispatches
  19: #     it STRAIGHT to alpha_palcode():
  20: #         case 0x00:  ic->f = instr(call_pal);  ic->arg[0] = iword & 0x3ffffff;
  21: #     so `call_pal 0x30' is literally the instruction word 0x00000030.
  22: #
  23: #     *** BUT "NO GATING OF ANY KIND" WAS MY CLAIM AND IT IS WRONG.  A seat corrected it, and
  24: #     the correction is the difference between a working probe and one that mysteriously
  25: #     halts. ***  Dispatch INTO alpha_palcode() carries no privilege check -- but the function
  26: #     GATES ITS OWN SWITCH, at cpu_alpha_palcode.c:183:
  27: #
  28: #         uint64_t userMode = cpu->cd.alpha.ps & ALPHA_PSL_USERMODE;   /* 0x0008 */
  29: #         if (userMode && !(palcode == 0x80 || 0x81 || 0x83 || 0xae ||
  30: #                           0xaa || 0x86 || 0x9e || 0x92 || 0x9f)) {
  31: #                 fatal("[ Privileged Alpha PALcode called from user mode: TODO ]");
  32: #                 cpu->running = 0;          <-- THE MACHINE STOPS
  33: #                 return;
  34: #         }
  35: #
  36: #     0x30 is NOT on that whitelist.  So in user mode `call_pal 0x30' does not reach swpctx --
  37: #     it HALTS THE CPU.  **That failure would present as a probe whose step never completes,
  38: #     i.e. as a broken harness rather than as a mode problem**, which is exactly why it gets a
  39: #     control row of its own below rather than a comment.
  40: #
  41: #     `cpu->cd.alpha.ps' has no explicit initialiser anywhere in src/, so it is zero from the
  42: #     zeroed cpu struct and USERMODE is clear -- kernel mode.  **That is a DERIVATION, not a
  43: #     measurement**, and the `palgate' row is what turns it into one.
  44: #
  45: #  3. THE MEMORY-FORMAT ENCODING is opcode<<26 | ra<<21 | rb<<16 | disp16, and the opcodes are
  46: #     read off cpu_alpha_instr.c's own decoder rather than from a manual: LDA 0x08, LDAH 0x09,
  47: #     LDQ 0x29, STQ 0x2d, LDL 0x28, STL 0x2c.
  48: #
  49: #  4. REGISTER NUMBERS AND DEBUGGER NAMES come from cpu_alpha.h's ALPHA_REG_NAMES:
  50: #     r0 v0, r1 t0, r2 t1, r3 t2, ... r16 a0, ... r30 sp, r31 zero.
  51: #
  52: #  *** WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and is exactly what the controls are for: WHICH ADDRESSES ARE
  53: #  WRITABLE RAM.  The machine loads a file at 0x20000000 and `alphaserver4100'/`eb164' report
  54: #  "cpu0: starting at 0x0000000020000000", but -M 64 is 64 MB and 0x20000000 is 512 MB, so the
  55: #  two facts do not obviously agree.  THIS FILE DOES NOT GUESS: the liveness control writes a
  56: #  known value through a real guest store and reads it back through a real guest load, and if
  57: #  the address is wrong that row goes RED and says so.  A harness that assumed an address and
  58: #  then reported a defect would be measuring its own mistake. ***
  59: 
  60: import os, sys, re, pty, time, select
  61: 
  62: BIN   = sys.argv[1]
  63: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
  64: MACH  = sys.argv[3] if len(sys.argv) > 3 else "alphabook1"
  65: 
  66: rows = []
  67: 
  68: def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
  69:     ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
  70:     rows.append((name, ok))
  71:     print("%-18s %-20s %-20s %-40s %s" % (name, str(got), ("NOT " if negate else "") + str(want),
  72:                                           note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
  73:     return ok
  74: 
  75: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the encoder
  76: #  Memory format.  `disp' is a SIGNED 16-bit displacement, so it is masked here and the caller
  77: #  is responsible for keeping values in range -- an out-of-range displacement silently truncating
  78: #  would be a wrong instruction that still assembles, which is the hand-assembled-encoding trap
  79: #  this project has paid for four times.  Hence the assert.
  80: def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
 150:     try:
 151:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
 152:     except OSError:
 153:         pass
 154:     try:
 155:         os.close(fd)
 156:     except OSError:
 157:         pass
 158:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive
 159: 
 160: def printed(t, reg):
 161:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 162:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)
 163: 
 164: def run_ops(ops, at=0x01010000):
 165:     put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
 166:     return put + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(ops)]
 167: 
 168: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- go
 169: DUMMY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")
 170: if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
 171:     with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
 172:         f.write(b"\x00" * 8)
 227: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the swpctx witness
 228: #  *** THE FIRST DESIGN WAS SELF-DEFEATING AND A SEAT REFUTED IT.  The defect DESTROYS THE PATH
 229: #  THE WITNESS NEEDS TO OBSERVE IT, and that is why everything below lives in K0SEG. ***
 230: #
 231: #  The naive design -- put both PCBs in ordinary RAM, swpctx twice, read ctx_A+16 -- fails because
 232: #  `alpha_translate_v2p' only identity-maps while `base == 0':
 233: #
 234: #      if (!userMode && (vaddr >= ALPHA_K0SEG_BASE && vaddr <= ALPHA_K0SEG_END)) {
 235: #              *return_paddr = vaddr & 0x000003ffffffffffULL;   /* checked FIRST */
 236: #              return 2; }
 237: #      ...
 238: #      if (!userMode && base == 0) { *return_paddr = vaddr & ...; ... }   /* the "UGLY hack" */
 239: #
 240: #  The moment the buggy line makes `apcb_ptbr' nonzero, `base != 0', the hack stops applying, and
 241: #  every later access -- INCLUDING INSTRUCTION FETCH -- falls into a page-table walk over garbage.
 242: #  One vaddr in that path even has an `exit(1)'.  So the second `call_pal' never lands and the save
 243: #  that is supposed to publish the defect never happens.
 244: #
 245: #  AND THE OBVIOUS FALLBACK FAILS TOO, by symmetry: putting the marker in `ptbr' instead of `flags'
 246: #  makes the identity map die on the FIXED build rather than on the defective one.  **No nonzero
 247: #  value anywhere in that field survives either polarity**, so K0SEG is not the better option -- it
 248: #  is the only one.
 249: #
 250: #  K0SEG is checked BEFORE `base' is read, so a K0SEG access is immune to whatever `apcb_ptbr'
 251: #  holds.  Code, both PCBs and the pointer vector therefore all live there.
 252: #
 253: #  *** THE BOOTSTRAP, because lda/ldah build 32-bit constants and K0SEG needs bit 63..42: ***
 254: #  the 64-bit pointers are written into memory with the debugger's `put d' (64-bit; note `put q' is
 255: #  128-bit in this debugger) and the guest loads them with ONE identity-mapped `ldq' issued while
 256: #  `ptbr' is still zero.  After that every address the guest uses is a K0SEG value held in a
 257: #  register, and nothing depends on translation state again.
 258: K0SEG      = 0xfffffc0000000000
 259: def k0(p):  return K0SEG | p
 260: 
 261: #  *** EVERY ADDRESS HERE WAS OUTSIDE RAM UNTIL A CODE REVIEW SAID SO, AND THE FAILURE WOULD
 262: #  HAVE BEEN SILENT AND GREEN. ***  The machine is built with `-M 64' = 64 MB, and these were
 263: #  at 0x200xxxxx (512 MB+).  K0SEG translation is `vaddr & 0x3ffffffffff', so the K0SEG idea
 264: #  survives contact with alpha_translate_v2p -- but the PHYSICAL BACKING does not.
 265: #
 266: #  `memory_warn_about_unimplemented_addr' only halts when `halt_on_nonexistant_memaccess' is
 267: #  set, i.e. under `-T'.  WITHOUT IT: writes succeed and are DISCARDED, and reads RETURN
 268: #  ZEROS.  An instruction fetch of zeros is a real instruction -- `call_pal 0'.
 269: #
 270: #  So with the old plan the guest wrote nothing, read zeros, and the defect row -- which
 271: #  wanted ZERO for the correct case -- would have gone GREEN while measuring "unmapped reads
 272: #  return 0".  Three rows were exposed to that.
 273: P_CODE, P_PTRV, P_PCB_A, P_PCB_B = 0x01040000, 0x01020000, 0x01030000, 0x01031000
 274: assert max(P_CODE, P_PTRV, P_PCB_A, P_PCB_B) < 64*1024*1024, \
 275:     'address outside the 64 MB the machine is built with -- reads would return 0 silently'
 276: #  *** TWO DISTINCTIVE VALUES, AND NEITHER IS ZERO.  THIS IS THE POINT. ***
 277: #  The first design put 0 in A.ptbr so the CORRECT case read back 0 -- and 0 is also what an
 278: #  unmapped read returns, so a broken setup was indistinguishable from a correct handler.
 279: #  K0SEG is what makes the repair possible: translation never consults `apcb_ptbr' for a
 280: #  K0SEG address, so `apcb_ptbr' may hold anything without breaking the probe.
 281: #
 282: #      correct handler  -> PCB_A+16 == PTBR_VAL   (apcb_ptbr keeps A's ptbr)
 283: #      the defect       -> PCB_A+16 == FLAGS_VAL  (apcb_ptbr took A's flags)
 284: #      anything broken  -> 0, which is NEITHER, so BOTH rows red
 285: PTBR_VAL  = 0x00000000aaaa1111     # A.ptbr
 286: FLAGS_VAL = 0x00000000bbbb2222     # A.flags
 287: MARKER = FLAGS_VAL                  # kept for the row text below
 288: 
 289: #  PCB field offsets, read off the save block in cpu_alpha_palcode.c:
 290: #      +0 ksp  +8 usp  +16 ptbr  +24 cpc(32)  +28 asn(32)  +32 unique  +40 flags  +48/56 decrsv
 291: def pcb(base, ptbr, flags):
 292:     return ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 0,  0),
 293:             "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 8,  0),
 294:             "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 16, ptbr),
 295:             "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 32, 0),
 296:             "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 40, flags),
 297:             "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 48, 0),
 298:             "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (base + 56, 0)]
 299: 
 300: #  A.ptbr = 0 so the CORRECT build leaves apcb_ptbr zero; A.flags = MARKER so the DEFECTIVE build
 301: #  copies MARKER into apcb_ptbr.  The second swpctx then saves apcb_ptbr into A+16, and that word
 302: #  is the oracle: 0 if correct, MARKER if the defect is present.
 303: setup = pcb(P_PCB_A, PTBR_VAL, FLAGS_VAL) + pcb(P_PCB_B, 0, 0)
 304: setup += ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (P_PTRV + 0,  k0(P_PCB_A)),
 305:           "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (P_PTRV + 8,  k0(P_PCB_B)),
 306:           "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (P_PTRV + 16, k0(P_PTRV))]
 307: 
 308: A0 = 16
 309: prog = (load_const(T0, P_PTRV)          # identity-mapped, while ptbr is still 0
 310:         + [LDQ(T0, 16, T0),             # t0 = K0SEG alias of the pointer vector
 311:            LDQ(A0, 0, T0),              # a0 = &PCB_A  (K0SEG)
 312:            LDQ(T3, 0, T0),              # t3 = &PCB_A  kept for the readback
 313:            CALL_PAL(0x30),              # swpctx -> loads A; the defect sets apcb_ptbr = MARKER
 314:            LDQ(A0, 8, T0),              # a0 = &PCB_B  -- t0 is K0SEG, so this survives
 315:            CALL_PAL(0x30),              # swpctx -> SAVES apcb_ptbr into PCB_A+16
 316:            LDQ(T2, 16, T3),             # t2 = that saved word
 317:            LDQ(T4, 40, T3)])            # t4 = the saved FLAGS word (`swpctx flags')
 318: 
 319: code_cmds = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog)]
 320: t, alive = session(setup + code_cmds + ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog),
 321:                                         "print t2", "print t4"])
 322: got = printed(t, "t2") if alive else "died"
 323: 
 324: row("swpctx alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive",
 325:     "the guest survives two swpctx calls in K0SEG")
 326: 
 327: #  THE DEFECT ROW.  RED on the committed build (it reads MARKER), GREEN once :282 assigns
 328: #  apcb_flags instead of apcb_ptbr.
 329: row("swpctx ptbr", got, "0x%x" % PTBR_VAL,
 330:     "PCB_A+16 must hold A.ptbr, not A.flags")
 331: 
 332: #  CLAUSE (b): pins the SPECIFIC wrong value, so a reinstatement of the defect is distinguishable
 333: #  from any other way of getting the word wrong.
 334: #  *** AN INEQUALITY PASSES ON EVERY NON-OBSERVATION.  Repaired 2026-09-07 after a probe-helper
 335: #  audit found this row, and four siblings across three probes, green on "absent"/"died"/0x0 --
 336: #  states in which nothing was measured at all.  `swpctx ptbr' reds on those, so the DETECTOR
 337: #  never escaped; what was wrong was this row's own note, which claims a control it did not have.
 338: #  Stated positively, a non-observation lands in the `got' column instead of scoring green. ***
 339: obs = got.startswith("0x") and got != "0x0"
 340: notflags = obs and got != ("0x%x" % FLAGS_VAL)
 341: row("swpctx notflags", "yes" if notflags else got, "yes",
 342:     "an OBSERVED word that is not A.flags -- absent/died/0x0 are setup failures, not passes")
 343: 
 344: #  AND NOT ZERO EITHER.  Zero is what an unmapped read returns, so it is the signature of a BROKEN
 345: #  SETUP rather than of either code state.  Without this row a probe whose addresses drifted back
 346: #  outside RAM would fail `swpctx ptbr' and pass `swpctx notflags', which reads like a real defect.
 347: #  *** THE SECOND CONSEQUENCE, and this witness was BLIND to it until 2026-09-07. ***
 348: #  `:282' assigns new_flags to apcb_ptbr, so apcb_flags is NEVER LOADED -- and the next SAVE then
 349: #  publishes that never-loaded word into the outgoing PCB, wiping the flags the guest planted.
 350: #
 351: #  A fix review put it plainly: both rows above read the SAME cell (A+16), so a build that merely
 352: #  stops clobbering PTBR -- delete `:282', or send new_flags to any other field -- scores a clean
 353: #  sweep.  This row reads A+40, on the page `swpctx mapped' already proved is backed: 0 on HEAD (a
 354: #  save of a field that was never loaded), FLAGS_VAL once the load is correct.
 355: row("swpctx flags", printed(t, "t4"), "0x%x" % FLAGS_VAL,
 356:     "PCB_A+40 must hold A.flags -- apcb_flags is never loaded on HEAD")
 357: 
 358: row("swpctx mapped", "yes" if obs else got, "yes",
 359:     "a real nonzero readback -- 0x0/absent/died all mean the memory was never there")
 360: 
 361: ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
 362: print("")
 363: print("ALPHAHARNESS_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
 364: print("ALPHAHARNESS_DONE")
 365: sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)

 135: struct sgi_re_data {
 136: 	// Rendering engine registers:
 137: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_a[256];
 138: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_b[256];
 139: 	uint16_t	re_tlb_c[256];

 143: 	uint32_t	re_linear_b[32];
 144: 
 145: 	// Drawing engine registers:
 146: 	uint32_t	de_reg[DEV_SGI_DE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 147: 
 148: 	// Memory transfer engine registers:
 149: 	uint32_t	mte_reg[DEV_SGI_MTE_LENGTH / sizeof(uint32_t)];
 150: 
 151: 	/*
 152: 	 *  #497: width-guard decline latches; see the enum above.
 153: 	 *
 154: 	 *  PLACED LAST, DELIBERATELY, AND THE ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING.
 155: 	 *  re_linear_a/b are written at [tlbi] AND [tlbi+1] with tlbi able to
 156: 	 *  reach 31, so index 32 of either array lands on whichever field
 157: 	 *  FOLLOWS it.  An earlier draft of this change put `warned' between
 158: 	 *  re_linear_b and de_reg; measured with offsetof, that made
 159: 	 *  re_linear_b[32] alias `warned' rather than de_reg[0] -- which both
 160: 	 *  falsified the adjacency the two LINEAR site comments cite, and made
 161: 	 *  an over-run SET WARNING LATCH BITS and silence this device's own
 162: 	 *  diagnostics.  Keeping the latch after mte_reg restores the
 163: 	 *  documented adjacency: re_linear_a[32] is re_linear_b[0] and
 164: 	 *  re_linear_b[32] is de_reg[0].
 165: 	 */
 166: 	uint32_t	warned;
 167: };

 170: /*
 171:  *  horrible_getputpixel():
 172:  *
 173:  *  This routine gets/puts a pixel in one of the tiles, from the perspective of
 174:  *  the rendering/drawing engine. Given x and y, it figures out which tile
 175:  *  number it is, and then finally does a slow read/write to get/put the pixel
 176:  *  at the correct sub-coordinates within the tile.
 177:  *
 178:  *  Tiles are always 512 _bytes_ wide, and 128 pixels high. For 32-bit color
 179:  *  modes, for example, that means 128 x 128 pixels.
 180:  *
 181:  *  For "linear" modes, y is ignored and x is an offset to select which linear
 182:  *  TLB entry to use.
 183:  */
 184: void horrible_getputpixel(int put, struct cpu* cpu, struct sgi_re_data* d,
 185: 	int x, int y, uint32_t* color, int mode)
 186: {
 187: 	uint32_t color_mode = mode & DE_MODE_TYPE_MASK;
 188: 	int bufdepth = 1 << ((mode >> 8) & 3); 
 189: 
 190: 	// dst_mode (see NetBSD's crmfbreg.h):
 191: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_A           0x00000000
 192: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_B           0x00000400
 193: 	// #define DE_MODE_TLB_C           0x00000800
 194: 	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_A           0x00001000
 195: 	// #define DE_MODE_LIN_B           0x00001400
 196: 	uint32_t tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7;
 197: 	int linear = tlb_mode > 3;
 198: 
 199: 	if (!linear && (x < 0 || y < 0 || x >= 2048 || y >= 2048))
 200: 		return;
 201: 
 202: 	int tilewidth_in_pixels = 512 / bufdepth;
 203: 	
 204: 	int tile_nr_x = x / tilewidth_in_pixels;
 205: 	int tile_nr_y = y >> 7;
 206: 
 207: 	unsigned int tile_nr = tile_nr_y * 16 + tile_nr_x;
 208: 
 209: 	y &= 127;
 210: 	int xofs = (x % tilewidth_in_pixels) * bufdepth;
 211: 	int ofs = 512 * y + xofs;
 212: 
 213: 	uint32_t tileptr = 0;
 214: 
 215: 	/*  #100: cases 0-2 index re_tlb_[abc][256] with a guest-derived
 216: 	    tile_nr = (y>>7)*16 + x/tilewidth; bound it (case 4 self-checks).  */
 217: 	if (tlb_mode <= 2 && tile_nr >= 256)
 218: 		return;
 219: 
 220: 	switch (tlb_mode) {
 221: 	case 0:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_a[tile_nr] << 16;
 222: 		break;
 223: 	case 1:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_b[tile_nr] << 16;
 224: 		break;
 225: 	case 2:	tileptr = d->re_tlb_c[tile_nr] << 16;
 226: 		break;
 227: 	case 4:	tile_nr = x >> 12;
 228: 		if (tile_nr >= 32)
 229: 			return;
 230: 		tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[tile_nr] << 12);
 231: 		ofs = x & 4095;
 232: 		// TODO... probably not correct!
 233: 		// printf("tileptr = %08x x = %i y = %i tile_nr = %i ofs = %i\n", tileptr, x, y, tile_nr, ofs);
 234: 		break;
 235: 	default:/*  sgirandompixel: this arm used to assign `*color = random()' -- with
 236: 		    its exit(1) commented out -- so a failed GET handed the CALLER a
 237: 		    random value, and draw_primitive() then wrote it to the destination
 238: 		    in the same loop iteration, since `color' is shared by the XFER GET
 239: 		    and the PUT.  DE_MODE_LIN_B (0x1400) is one of the FIVE modes
 240: 		    crmfbreg.h documents and extracts to tlb_mode 5, so this was
 241: 		    reachable with a DOCUMENTED mode, not only with a junk one.
 242: 		    MEASURED, rung 3: the destination received 0x4b1763a1, 0x2ee4b4df
 243: 		    and 0x7433cdc1 on successive runs of
 244: 		    regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py.
 245: 
 246: 		    NOW DECLINED: `*color' is left alone, so the caller's own
 247: 		    `uint32_t color = fg' stands and what lands is at least
 248: 		    DETERMINISTIC.  The announce is latched because this helper is
 249: 		    per-pixel.
 250: 
 251: 		    WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, filed rather than silently omitted: the
 252: 		    caller STILL PERFORMS its dependent PUT, so a declined GET still
 253: 		    writes fg (row sgirandomskipput -- suppressing it needs a return
 254: 		    value and every GET site changed, which is more than one site);
 255: 		    LIN_B is still not implemented, and the panel refused to endorse
 256: 		    implementing it as LIN_A-with-the-other-array on symmetry alone
 257: 		    (row sgirelinb); and the COLOUR-mode switch below carries the same
 258: 		    random() shape at its own default (row sgirandomcolour).  */
 259: 		if (!(d->warned & SGIRE_W_BADMODE)) {
 260: 			d->warned |= SGIRE_W_BADMODE;
 261: 			/*  `dst_mode' is kept from the original text although the
 262: 			    parameter is also passed as src_mode on a GET: the probe
 263: 			    anchors on this literal, and rewording it here would be
 264: 			    scope creep AND would move an oracle anchor mid-round,
 265: 			    which is the laundering shape whatever the intent.
 266: 			    Filed as sgirenamedstmode.  */
 267: 			fatal("[ sgi_re: unimplemented dst_mode %i for "
 268: 			    "horrible_getputpixel (%s), x=%i y=%i; declined "
 269: 			    "(once) ]\n", mode, put ? "put" : "get", x, y);
 270: 		}
 271: 		return;
 272: 	}
 273: 
 274: 	// The highest bit seems to be set for a "valid" tile pointer.
 275: 	if (!(tileptr & 0x80000000)) {
 276: 		//printf("dst_mode %i, tile_nr = %i,  tileptr = 0x%llx\n", dst_mode, tile_nr, (long long)tileptr);
 277: 		//fatal("sgi gbe horrible_getputpixel: unexpected non-set high bit of tileptr?\n");
 278: 		//exit(1);
 279: 		return;
 280: 	}
 281: 	
 282: 	tileptr &= ~0x80000000;
 283: 
 284: 	uint8_t buf[8];		/* #100: bufdepth can be 8 = 1<<((mode>>8)&3) */
 285: 	if (put) {
 286: 		switch (color_mode) {
 287: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_CI:
 288: 			buf[0] = *color;
 289: 			break;
 290: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 291: 			buf[0] = *color >> 24;
 292: 			buf[1] = *color >> 16;
 293: 			buf[2] = *color >> 8;
 294: 			buf[3] = 0;
 295: 			break;
 296: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
 297: 			buf[0] = *color >> 24;
 298: 			buf[1] = *color >> 16;
 299: 			buf[2] = *color >> 8;
 300: 			buf[3] = *color;
 301: 			break;
 302: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR:
 303: 			buf[0] = *color;
 304: 			buf[1] = *color >> 8;
 305: 			buf[2] = *color >> 16;
 306: 			buf[3] = *color >> 24;
 307: 			break;
 308: 		default:buf[0] = random();
 309: 			buf[1] = random();
 310: 			buf[2] = random();
 311: 			buf[3] = random();
 312: 			// TODO.
 313: 			fatal("[ put: color mode = 0x%x ]\n", color_mode);
 314: 		}
 315: 
 316: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
 317: 		    buf, bufdepth, MEM_WRITE, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
 318: 	} else {
 319: 		cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, tileptr + ofs,
 320: 		    buf, bufdepth, MEM_READ, NO_EXCEPTIONS | PHYSICAL);
 321: 
 322: 		switch (color_mode) {
 323: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_CI:
 324: 			*color = buf[0];
 325: 			break;
 326: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 327: 			/*  #505: this read buf[1..3] while the PUT arm above
 328: 			    writes the three channels at buf[0..2] and an
 329: 			    explicit pad at buf[3] -- so an RGB pixel did not
 330: 			    survive its own round trip, shifting one byte left
 331: 			    with a zero fill on every RGB source read.
 332: 
 333: 			    The PUT arm is the authoritative one, and the
 334: 			    warrant is OUTSIDE this function: dev_sgi_gbe.c's
 335: 			    scanout reads the same tile memory back for display
 336: 			    as (buf[0]<<24)+(buf[1]<<16)+(buf[2]<<8)+buf[3],
 337: 			    then CRMFB_MODE_TYP_RGB8 takes R,G,B from bits
 338: 			    31..24, 23..16 and 15..8 -- byte 0 is R and byte 3
 339: 			    is UNUSED.  So the pad sits at buf[3] because the
 340: 			    consumer ignores buf[3], not because PUT writes a
 341: 			    zero there; that is what makes this non-circular.
 342: 			    The RGBA and ABGR PUT arms independently agree --
 343: 			    both lay [R,G,B,x] in memory despite opposite
 344: 			    register word orders -- which leaves this GET the
 345: 			    lone container outlier among the 32-bit arms.
 346: 
 347: 			    The low byte stays ZERO on purpose: RGB carries
 348: 			    three channels, so a corrected read of 0xaabbccdd
 349: 			    yields 0xaabbcc00, NOT the source word.  */
 350: 			*color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8);
 351: 			break;
 352: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGBA:
 353: 			*color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8) + buf[3];
 354: 			break;
 355: 		case DE_MODE_TYPE_ABGR:
 356: 			*color = (buf[3] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[1] << 8) + buf[0];
 357: 			break;
 358: 		default:// Read "raw" 32-bit value:
 359: 			*color = (buf[3] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[1] << 8) + buf[0];
 360: 			fatal("[ get: color mode = 0x%x ]\n", color_mode);
 361: 		}
 362: 	}
 363: }

 370: DEVICE_ACCESS(sgi_re)
 371: {
 372: 	struct sgi_re_data *d = (struct sgi_re_data *) extra;
 373: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;

 377: 	relative_addr += 0x1000;
 378: 
 379: 	/*
 380: 	 *  #497: THE SIX WIDTH GUARDS BELOW USED TO END THE HOST PROCESS.
 381: 	 *
 382: 	 *  A guest choosing an access width is not an emulator error, so each
 383: 	 *  `len != 8' arm now DECLINES the access and announces itself once.
 384: 	 *  Three properties of the shape are load-bearing:
 385: 	 *
 386: 	 *  1. It is an `else if' SPLICE, not an early return.  A `return' here
 387: 	 *     would skip this function's common tail, and `return 0' would be
 388: 	 *     coerced to -1 by cpus/memory_rw.c and raise EXCEPTION_DBE in the
 389: 	 *     guest -- turning a declined access into a guest bus error.
 390: 	 *
 391: 	 *  2. Control therefore reaches the tail, which answers a declined READ
 392: 	 *     with odata (0).
 393: 	 *
 394: 	 *     *** THIS PARAGRAPH ONCE CALLED THAT TAIL DEAD CODE -- "every read arm
 395: 	 *     below still exit(1)s, so odata is never assigned today".  #499 MADE IT

 397: 	 *     read now runs the tail and lands a defined 0, and the shipped detector's
 398: 	 *     `rd sent' row exists to pin exactly that.  The six `TODO: read from'
 399: 	 *     arms below DO still exit(1) -- that half is unchanged and is sgirekills. ***
 400: 	 *
 401: 	 *  3. The guards are NOT redundant.  For TLB_A/B/C and TEX the store is a
