#!/usr/bin/env python3
#
#  alphapfnoffset -- RUNG-3 WITNESS: the Alpha page-table walk drops the within-page offset.
#
#      memory_alpha.c   (*return_paddr) = ALPHA_PTE_TO_PFN(pte3) << pageSizeShift;
#      memory_rw.c      offset = paddr & offset_mask;
#
#  The walk returns a PAGE BASE, so `paddr & offset_mask' is zero and a walked access resolves to
#  the page's first bytes.  A codex reachability pass enumerated all 8192 offsets: the walk yields
#  offset zero throughout, differing from the offset-preserving address for 8191 of them.
#
#  *** THIS IS A WITNESS (rung 3), NOT A DETECTOR.  It shows the symptom on the COMMITTED build,
#  which is what licenses a fix, and it must NOT be gated -- a witness stays red until the fix
#  lands.  Its rows therefore assert the CORRECT behaviour and are expected to FAIL here. ***
#
#  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
#  SIX THINGS THE DESIGN REVIEW (grok, 2026-09-07) ESTABLISHED.  Four are traps that would have
#  produced a GREEN run on the defective build; none is obvious from the source.
#
#  1. THE SWAP IS NOT A BLOCKER.  `swpctx' is the only writer of apcb_ptbr -- but it is only a
#     CONFOUND when ptbr and flags DIFFER.  Put the SAME L1 PFN in both PCB fields and the
#     defective line and the corrected line both leave apcb_ptbr = L1_PFN.  (The inverse is a
#     trap: PFN in flags with ptbr = 0 arms the walk on HEAD and leaves IDENTITY MAPPING on a
#     fixed PAL, so post-fix green would be the bootstrap, not the repair.)
#
#  2. PFN, NOT BYTE ADDRESS.  apcb_ptbr and the PTE high halves hold PFNs.  ptbr = 0x01050000
#     would give base = 0x01050000 << 13, past RAM, and the walk reports `wasn't found'.
#
#  3. ONE COLD SHOT.  The fast path caches the page from `paddr & ~offset_mask' -- the CORRECT
#     physical page -- so the walked paddr is wrong only for the FILL.  A second access at any
#     offset is a hit and reads the right bytes, i.e. GOES GREEN ON HEAD.  Load once; never retry.
#
#  4. THE POLARITY TRAP.  Planting B *through the walk* makes HEAD store it at data+0 and read it
#     back from data+0 -- the defect measured against itself, green.  A cousin: planting at vaddr
#     0/N while ptbr == 0 hits PHYSICAL 0/N, not the data page.  So A and B are planted through
#     K0SEG of the DATA PAGE, before the walk is ever used.
#
#  5. A FAILED WALK DOES NOT WRITE THE DESTINATION REGISTER.  LS_GENERIC_N returns on a memory_rw
#     failure without storing, so "faults or returns zero" is false.  Hence a THREE-WAY oracle
#     with a sentinel S pre-loaded into the destination:
#         S = the walk never completed        0 = walked onto unmapped physical
#         A = right page, offset dropped      B = correct
#
#  6. ONLY ALPHA_PTE_VALID IS LOAD-BEARING.  Verified in memory_alpha.c: each level tests
#     `!(pteN & ALPHA_PTE_VALID)' and nothing else -- not KR/KW/UR/UW, FAULT_ON_*, ASM or
#     granularity.  Setting bits the walk does not implement and calling them required would be
#     the "cannot hurt" addition this project refuses.
#
#  Usage:  alpha_pfnoffset_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
import os, sys, pty, select, time, re

BIN   = sys.argv[1]
LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
MACH  = "alphabook1"

rows = []
def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
    ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
    rows.append((name, ok))
    shown = ("NOT " + str(want)) if negate else str(want)
    print("%-14s %-20s %-22s %-44s %s" % (name, str(got), shown, note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
    return ok

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- encoder
ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3, T4 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5
A0 = 16

def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
    assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
    return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)

def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
def CALL_PAL(n):             return n & 0x3ffffff

def load_const(reg, val):
    lo, hi = val & 0xffff, (val >> 16) & 0xffff
    if lo & 0x8000:
        hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
    return [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000),
            LDA (reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg)]

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the map
K0SEG      = 0xfffffc0000000000
def k0(p): return K0SEG | p

PAGESHIFT  = 13
PTE_VALID  = 0x1
def pte(pfn): return (pfn << 32) | PTE_VALID

#  Off the PROM pocket (0x10000 entry stub, 0x10100 initial ctx, 0x12000 argspace, 0x14000 HWRPB)
#  and off the sibling probes' 0x0102/0103/0104xxxx scratch.
P_L1, P_L2, P_L3, P_DATA = 0x01050000, 0x01052000, 0x01054000, 0x01056000
P_PCB, P_CODE            = 0x01058000, 0x0105a000
P_DATA2                  = 0x0105c000          # mapped at vaddr 0x2000 via L3[1]
L1_PFN = P_L1 >> PAGESHIFT

#  -M 64.  A page past physical_max is written to nothing and read back as zero, SILENTLY.
assert max(P_L1, P_L2, P_L3, P_DATA, P_DATA2, P_PCB, P_CODE) < 64 * 1024 * 1024
assert all(p % 8192 == 0 for p in (P_L1, P_L2, P_L3, P_DATA, P_DATA2)), "pages must be page-aligned"

VADDR = 0x0000            # i1 = i2 = i3 = 0
N     = 8                 # aligned: an unaligned N forces the generic path for the WRONG reason
VAL_A = 0xaaaa0000aaaa    # planted at data + 0   -- what the DEFECT returns
VAL_B = 0xbbbb0000bbbb    # planted at data + N   -- what a CORRECT walk returns
#  ---- the SECOND cold row: non-zero VPN, offset in the UPPER 4 KB ----
VADDR2 = 0x2000           # i1 = i2 = 0, i3 = 1  -- the VPN is NON-ZERO, which is the point
N2     = 0x1008           # upper 4 KB, 8-aligned
VAL_A2 = 0xcccc0000cccc   # data2 + 0        <- what a page-base result reads
VAL_B2 = 0xdddd0000dddd   # data2 + 0x1008   <- CORRECT
VAL_C2 = 0xeeee0000eeee   # data2 + 0x0008   <- what a 0xfff mask, or `| 8', reads
assert (VADDR2 >> 13) == 1, "VADDR2 must select L3[1] -- a NON-ZERO VPN is the whole point"
assert N2 >= 0x1000 and N2 % 8 == 0, "N2 must sit in the upper 4 KB and be 8-aligned"

VAL_S = 0x55000055        # sentinel: unchanged means the load never stored.  32-bit with
                          # bit 31 CLEAR, because load_const builds it -- a wider value
                          # would silently truncate and the row would compare the wrong thing.

DUMMY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")
if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
    with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
        f.write(b"\0" * 8)

def session(cmds, settle=1.6, spin=2.4):
    pid, fd = pty.fork()
    if pid == 0:
        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
                       "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
        os._exit(127)
    buf = ""
    def rd(t):
        nonlocal buf
        end = time.time() + t
        while time.time() < end:
            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
            if not r:
                continue
            try:
                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
                if not d:
                    return False
                buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
            except OSError:
                return False
        return True
    alive = rd(settle)
    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
        try:
            os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
        except OSError:
            alive = False; break
        if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.3):
            alive = False; break
    try:
        os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
    except OSError:
        pass
    try:
        os.close(fd)
    except OSError:
        pass
    return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive

def printed(t, reg):
    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
    return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)

print("alpha_pfnoffset_probe -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH))
print("%-14s %-20s %-22s %-44s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))

#  ---- setup: tables, data and the PCB, ALL planted while ptbr is still 0 ----------------------
#  K0SEG for the data page (trap 4); the table pages are equally reachable either way.
setup = [
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L1),        pte(P_L2 >> PAGESHIFT)),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L2),        pte(P_L3 >> PAGESHIFT)),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L3),        pte(P_DATA >> PAGESHIFT)),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA + 0),  VAL_A),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA + N),  VAL_B),
    #  the second page, and L3[1] -> it.  Entry 1 is at byte offset 8 of the L3 page.
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_L3 + 8),    pte(P_DATA2 >> PAGESHIFT)),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA2 + 0),      VAL_A2),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA2 + 8),      VAL_C2),
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_DATA2 + N2),     VAL_B2),
    #  PCB: ptbr AND flags both the L1 PFN, so the swpctx defect cannot change what gets armed.
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_PCB + 16),  L1_PFN),     # apcb_ptbr
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_PCB + 40),  L1_PFN),     # apcb_flags
    "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_CODE - 8),  k0(P_PCB)),  # a0 source for swpctx
]

#  ---- SESSION 1: the K0SEG controls, before any walk ------------------------------------------
#  T0 must hold a K0SEG address, which load_const CANNOT build (it makes 32-bit sign-extended
#  constants and K0SEG needs bits 63..42).  So the pointer is planted in memory and read with ldq,
#  which is the same bootstrap the two sibling Alpha probes use.
cmds = setup + ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (k0(P_CODE - 16), k0(P_DATA))]
prog_ctl = (load_const(T3, P_CODE - 16) +
            [LDQ(T0, 0, T3),      # t0 = k0(P_DATA)
             LDQ(T1, 0, T0),      # t1 = *(data + 0)   -- must be A
             LDQ(T2, N, T0)])     # t2 = *(data + N)   -- must be B
cmds += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_ctl)]
cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_ctl), "print t1", "print t2"]
t1txt, alive1 = session(cmds)

row("data0 k0", printed(t1txt, "t1"), "0x%x" % VAL_A,
    "CONTROL: A really is at data+0")
row("dataN k0", printed(t1txt, "t2"), "0x%x" % VAL_B,
    "CONTROL: B really is at data+N")

#  ---- SESSION 2: arm the walk, then ONE cold walked load --------------------------------------
prog_walk = (load_const(T3, P_CODE - 8) +
             [LDQ(A0, 0, T3),               # a0 = k0(P_PCB)
              CALL_PAL(0x30)] +             # swpctx -> apcb_ptbr = L1_PFN
             load_const(T4, VAL_S) +
             [LDQ(T4, VADDR + N, ZERO)])    # THE ONE COLD WALKED LOAD
cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_walk)]
cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_walk), "print t4"]
t2txt, alive2 = session(cmds)

dest     = printed(t2txt, "t4")
notfound = "wasn't found" in t2txt

#  ---- SESSION 3: the second cold load, non-zero VPN, offset in the upper 4 KB ---------------
#  A SEPARATE session because the load must be COLD: the fast path caches the correct physical
#  page, so a second access in the same run is a hit and reads the right bytes on HEAD.
prog_walk2 = (load_const(T3, P_CODE - 8) +
              [LDQ(A0, 0, T3), CALL_PAL(0x30)] +
              load_const(T4, VAL_S) +
              [LDQ(T4, VADDR2 + N2, ZERO)])
cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(P_CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_walk2)]
cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(P_CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_walk2), "print t4"]
t3txt, alive3 = session(cmds)
dest2 = printed(t3txt, "t4")

row("walk found", "absent" if not notfound else "wasn't found", "absent",
    "CONTROL: the table walked to a valid L3")
row("walk stored", dest, "0x%x" % VAL_S, negate=True,
    note="CONTROL: the sentinel surviving means the load never wrote t4 at all")
row("walk mapped", dest, "0x0", negate=True,
    note="CONTROL: 0 would mean the walk landed on unmapped physical")
row("walk off", dest, "0x%x" % VAL_B,
    "the walked load must return data+N, not data+0")

#  *** THE SECOND COLD ROW.  Six lesser edits score 6/6 on the row above; three of them die
#  here, and the three-way plant says WHICH: ***
#      VAL_B2  correct
#      VAL_C2  a 0xfff mask (the 4 KB copy) or a hardcoded `| 8'
#      VAL_A2  a page-base result
#  0x3fff and 0x7fff are EQUIVALENT inside an 8 KB page -- bits 13/14 of an offset are always
#  zero there -- so no row kills them and none should pretend to.
row("walk vpn", dest2, "0x%x" % VAL_B2,
    "non-zero VPN, offset in the UPPER 4 KB: kills the 0xfff mask and unmasked +vaddr")

ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
print()
print("ALPHAPFN_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
print("ALPHAPFN_DONE")
sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)

15	1	src/cpus/memory_alpha.c
11	2	src/devices/dev_lca.c
24	1	src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c

 succeeded in 205ms:
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: #
   3: #  lcaconfwidth -- RUNG-3 WITNESS: the LCA IOC_CONF kill diagnostic names a value the guard ACCEPTS.
   4: #
   5: #  THE DEFECT, dev_lca.c, `case LCA_IOC_CONF:'
   6: #
   7: #      d->ioc_conf = idata;                     /* the FULL 64-bit value is stored   */
   8: #      if (idata & ~1) {                        /* the guard sees all 64 bits        */
   9: #              fatal("TODO: Write to unimplemented bit of"
  10: #                  " IOC_CONF: 0x%x\n", (int)idata);   /* the message sees 32        */
  11: #              exit(1);
  12: #      }
  13: #
  14: #  A guest `stq' of 0x100000000 sets bit 32.  `idata & ~1' is true, so the process dies -- and
  15: #  `(int)idata' is 0, which is a value this guard ACCEPTS.  The kill message names an acceptable
  16: #  value while killing on an unacceptable one.  That is the whole defect, and it is observable.
  17: #
  18: #  *** WHAT THIS PROBE IS AND IS NOT.  It is a WITNESS (rung 3): it shows the symptom on the
  19: #  COMMITTED build, which is what licenses a fix.  It is not a detector and must not be gated --
  20: #  a witness stays red until the fix lands. ***
  21: #
  22: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  23: #  FOUR THINGS THE DESIGN REVIEW (grok, 2026-09-07) KILLED BEFORE THIS WAS WRITTEN.  Each would
  24: #  have produced a green or an unreadable run, and none is obvious from the source alone:
  25: #
  26: #  1. `load_const' CANNOT BUILD EITHER OPERAND.  LDA/LDAH make 32-bit sign-extended constants.
  27: #     The K0SEG address needs bits 63..42 and the test value needs bit 32.  So both 64-bit words
  28: #     are planted in RAM with debugger `put d' and loaded with `ldq' while `ptbr' is still 0 --
  29: #     the same bootstrap alpha_harness_probe.py uses, and the only one that works.
  30: #
  31: #  2. DEBUGGER `dump' CANNOT BE THE READ-BACK.  `dump' calls memory_rw with NO_EXCEPTIONS, and
  32: #     LCA registers DM_DEFAULT rather than DM_READS_HAVE_NO_SIDE_EFFECTS, so the handler is
  33: #     SKIPPED and the caller gets the pre-zeroed buffer.  That is the UNMAPPED SIGNATURE PRODUCED
  34: #     BY A HIT -- a false negative that looks exactly like a miss.  The read-back is a guest `ldq'.
  35: #
  36: #  3. `put d' OF THE KILLING VALUE IS NOT RUNG-3.  store_64bit_word goes through memory_rw with
  37: #     CACHE_DATA and no NO_EXCEPTIONS, so it DOES enter the handler and exit(1)s -- a real kill,
  38: #     caused by no guest instruction.  The killing store must be a guest `stq'.
  39: #
  40: #  4. A MISS IS NOT ONLY "UNMAPPED -> NOTHING".  Offset 0 of the window is LCA_IOC_BASE (HAE),
  41: #     which `break's with no kill.  A store to k0(0x180000000) is SILENT WHILE INSIDE the LCA
  42: #     window.  Unmapped and HAE both read back 0, so the control has to tell them apart -- which
  43: #     is what `ioc rt' does: only the real IOC_CONF arm stores AND returns the value.
  44: #
  45: #  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  46: #  HONEST LIMIT, stated because the row must not claim a language guarantee: `(int)0x100000000 == 0'
  47: #  is IMPLEMENTATION-DEFINED, not C99.  GCC and Clang truncate to the low 32 bits, which is what
  48: #  this tree is built with, so the witness holds on this build and is not a statement about C.
  49: #
  50: #  Usage:  lca_confwidth_probe.py <gxemul-binary> [label]
  51: import os, sys, pty, select, time, re, struct
  52: 
  53: BIN   = sys.argv[1]
  54: LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
  55: MACH  = "alphabook1"          #  device_add(machine, "lca") is ST_ALPHABOOK1 ONLY
  56: 
  57: rows = []
  58: def row(name, got, want, note="", negate=False):
  59:     ok = (got != want) if negate else (got == want)
  60:     rows.append((name, ok))
  61:     shown = ("NOT " + str(want)) if negate else str(want)
  62:     print("%-16s %-22s %-24s %-46s %s" % (name, str(got), shown, note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
  63:     return ok
  64: 
  65: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- encoder (memory format only)
  66: ZERO, T0, T1, T2, T3 = 31, 1, 2, 3, 4
  67: 
  68: def mem(op, ra, rb, disp):
  69:     assert -32768 <= disp <= 32767, "displacement %d out of range" % disp
  70:     return (op << 26) | (ra << 21) | (rb << 16) | (disp & 0xffff)
  71: 
  72: def LDA (ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x08, ra, rb, disp)
  73: def LDAH(ra, disp, rb=ZERO): return mem(0x09, ra, rb, disp)
  74: def LDQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x29, ra, rb, disp)
  75: def STQ (ra, disp, rb):      return mem(0x2d, ra, rb, disp)
  76: 
  77: def load_const(reg, val):
  78:     #  32-bit only, and deliberately so: this builds the SCRATCH pointer, never a 64-bit operand.
  79:     lo = val & 0xffff
  80:     hi = (val >> 16) & 0xffff
  81:     if lo & 0x8000:                      # LDA sign-extends its displacement
  82:         hi = (hi + 1) & 0xffff
  83:     ops = [LDAH(reg, hi if hi < 0x8000 else hi - 0x10000)]
  84:     ops.append(LDA(reg, lo if lo < 0x8000 else lo - 0x10000, reg))
  85:     return ops
  86: 
  87: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- addresses (ALL RAM < 64 MB)
  88: K0SEG       = 0xfffffc0000000000
  89: def k0(p):  return K0SEG | p
  90: 
  91: LCA_IOC_BASE = 0x180000000
  92: LCA_IOC_CONF = LCA_IOC_BASE + 0x020          # alpha_lcareg.h
  93: SCRATCH      = 0x01020000                    # the three 64-bit words live here
  94: CODE         = 0x01040000
  95: LEGAL        = 0x1                           # 1 & ~1 == 0 -> accepted, no kill
  96: KILLER       = 0x100000000                   # bit 32 -> guard fires; (int) of it is 0
  97: 
  98: #  The same guard the Alpha harness needed: an address past physical_max is written to nothing and
  99: #  read back as zero, SILENTLY.  -M 64 gives 64 MB.
 100: assert max(SCRATCH, CODE) < 64 * 1024 * 1024, "scratch/code must be inside RAM"
 101: 
 102: DUMMY = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)), "alpha_dummy.bin")
 103: if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
 104:     with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
 105:         f.write(b"\0" * 8)
 106: 
 107: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- session
 108: def session(cmds, settle=1.6, spin=2.2):
 109:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 110:     if pid == 0:
 111:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "alpha", "-e", MACH, "-M", "64",
 112:                        "0x20000000:" + DUMMY])
 113:         os._exit(127)
 114:     buf = ""
 115:     def rd(t):
 116:         nonlocal buf
 117:         end = time.time() + t
 118:         while time.time() < end:
 119:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 120:             if not r:
 121:                 continue
 122:             try:
 123:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 124:                 if not d:
 125:                     return False
 126:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 127:             except OSError:
 128:                 return False
 129:         return True
 130:     alive = rd(settle)
 131:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 132:         try:
 133:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 134:         except OSError:
 135:             alive = False; break
 136:         if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.3):
 137:             alive = False; break
 138:     try:
 139:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)
 140:     except OSError:
 141:         pass
 142:     try:
 143:         os.close(fd)
 144:     except OSError:
 145:         pass
 146:     return buf.replace("\r", ""), alive
 147: 
 148: def printed(t, reg):
 149:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 150:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)

 152: print("lca_confwidth_probe -- binary=%s label=%s machine=%s" % (BIN, LABEL, MACH))
 153: print("%-16s %-22s %-24s %-46s %s" % ("row", "got", "want", "note", ""))
 154: 
 155: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- SESSION 1: liveness + control
 156: #  Everything up to and including the legal round-trip, WITHOUT the killing store, so these rows
 157: #  are readable on a build where the killing store would end the process.
 158: setup = ["put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 0,  k0(LCA_IOC_CONF)),
 159:          "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 8,  LEGAL),
 160:          "put d 0x%x, 0x%x" % (SCRATCH + 16, KILLER)]
 161: 
 162: prog_ctl = (load_const(T0, SCRATCH) +

 164:              LDQ(T2, 8, T0),        # t2 = 0x1

 166:              LDQ(T3, 0, T1)])       # guest read-back through the SAME decode
 167: cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_ctl)]
 168: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_ctl), "print t3"]
 169: t1txt, alive1 = session(cmds)
 170: 
 171: row("live", "alive" if alive1 else "dead", "alive",
 172:     "the control session survives the legal write")
 173: row("ioc rt", printed(t1txt, "t3"), "0x1",
 174:     "guest stq 0x1 then guest ldq through the SAME decode")
 175: 
 176: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- SESSION 2: the killing store
 177: #  The killing store is LAST because nothing runs after it.
 178: prog_kill = (load_const(T0, SCRATCH) +
 179:              [LDQ(T1, 0, T0),       # t1 = k0(LCA_IOC_CONF)
 180:               LDQ(T2, 16, T0),      # t2 = 0x100000000
 181:               STQ(T2, 0, T1)])      # THE KILLING STORE
 182: cmds = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (k0(CODE) + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(prog_kill)]
 183: cmds += ["pc=0x%x" % k0(CODE), "step %d" % len(prog_kill)]
 184: t2txt, alive2 = session(cmds)

 186: #  *** PARSE THE VALUE; DO NOT SUBSTRING-MATCH IT. ***
 187: #  These rows used `"IOC_CONF: 0x100000000" in text'.  A message of 0x1000000000 -- one extra
 188: #  literal zero, a plausible edit at this very site -- CONTAINS that string, so the row went
 189: #  GREEN on a wrong diagnostic, and the displayed `got' was manufactured from the boolean rather
 190: #  than read from the output.  `zero' had the mirror flaw (0x0abc contains 0x0).
 191: #  Found by a pass-2 review of this round, not by the round itself.
 192: m_ioc = re.search(r"IOC_CONF: (0x[0-9a-fA-F]+)", t2txt)
 193: hit   = m_ioc is not None

 195: shown = ("0x%x" % ioc) if m_ioc else "absent"
 196: zero  = (ioc == 0)
 197: full  = (ioc == KILLER)

 199: #  *** ORACLE POLARITY.  The ladder requires a witness to show the symptom on the PRE-FIX build and
 200: #  go GREEN post-fix WITH THE SAME SCRIPT.  So these rows assert the CORRECT behaviour and are
 201: #  expected to FAIL here.  The first draft of this probe asserted the DEFECT as the passing state
 202: #  and scored 6/6 on the committed build -- which reads as success and would have gone RED the
 203: #  moment the bug was fixed.  That is a detector-of-the-defect wearing a witness's clothes, and it
 204: #  is the opposite of what clause (i) asks for. ***
 205: row("kill fired", "yes" if hit else "no", "yes",
 206:     "CONTROL: the IOC_CONF guard announced -- not HAE, not unmapped, not a skipped handler")
 207: row("kill width", shown, "0x%x" % KILLER,
 208:     "the message must name the value that ACTUALLY killed")

 211: #  is "absent", which is != "0x0" and scored GREEN.  `kill fired' reds on that, so the detector
 212: #  did not escape; this row's note claimed a control it did not have. ***
 213: notzero = hit and ioc != 0
 214: row("kill notzero", "yes" if notzero else shown, "yes",

 217:     "CONTROL: exit(1) really ran -- distinguishes a kill from a silent decline")

   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: #
   3: #  sgirergbroundtrip -- RUNG-3 WITNESS for dev_sgi_re.c horrible_getputpixel()'s RGB arms.
   4: #
   5: #  COPIED from regress/sgi_re_randompixel_probe.py, whose session already drives an XFER copy
   6: #  through this helper on an unmodified `-E sgi -e ip32'.  Only the SOURCE COLOUR TYPE changes.
   7: #
   8: #  THE DEFECT: the RGB PUT and GET arms disagree about which byte holds which channel.
   9: #      PUT  buf[0] = c>>24;  buf[1] = c>>16;  buf[2] = c>>8;  buf[3] = 0;
  10: #      GET  *color = (buf[1]<<24) + (buf[2]<<16) + (buf[3]<<8);
  11: #  PUT writes the top three bytes at 0,1,2; GET reads 1,2,3.  RGB is the ONLY multi-byte mode
  12: #  that fails its own round trip -- RGBA and ABGR both round-trip, using OPPOSITE byte orders,
  13: #  which is why this is an internal inconsistency and not an endianness question.
  14: #
  15: #  WHY THE ORACLE IS A VALUE AND NOT AN INEQUALITY: an XFER copy GETs with src_mode and PUTs
  16: #  with dst_mode, which are INDEPENDENT guest registers.  With src RGB and dst RGBA the
  17: #  corruption is a computable one-byte left shift with a zero fill, so the row asserts an exact
  18: #  value on both the defective and the fixed build.  No uninitialised byte is read, so the
  19: #  `separate automatic objects have no architectural promise' objection that killed a
  20: #  neighbouring witness design does not apply here -- checked, not assumed.
  21: #
  22: #  (original header follows)
  23: #  sgirandompixel -- the probe this was copied from.
  24: #
  25: #  THE DEFECT.  horrible_getputpixel() extracts `tlb_mode = (mode >> 10) & 0x7' and switches on it.
  26: #  Cases 0, 1, 2 and 4 are implemented.  The `default:' arm prints, has its exit(1) COMMENTED OUT,
  27: #  and then executes `*color = random(); return;'.  So a failed GET does not decline -- it hands the
  28: #  caller a random 32-bit value.  In draw_primitive() the failed source GET and the destination PUT
  29: #  share one `color' variable in one loop iteration:
  30: #
  31: #      if (drawmode & DE_DRAWMODE_XFER_EN)
  32: #              horrible_getputpixel(0, ..., src_x, src_y, &color, src_mode);   // may set random()
  33: #      ...
  34: #      if (draw)
  35: #              horrible_getputpixel(1, ..., x, y, &color, dst_mode);           // writes it
  36: #
  37: #  AND THE UNHANDLED VALUE IS A DOCUMENTED MODE.  src/include/thirdparty/crmfbreg.h defines five:
  38: #    DE_MODE_TLB_A 0x0000 -> 0 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_A 0x1000 -> 4 handled
  39: #    DE_MODE_TLB_B 0x0400 -> 1 handled     DE_MODE_LIN_B 0x1400 -> 5 *** default -> random() ***
  40: #    DE_MODE_TLB_C 0x0800 -> 2 handled
  41: #
  42: #  CONTRACT (vacuity-floor clause (e)) -- what this witness catches and what it does NOT:
  43: #    CATCHES   the RGB GET arm re-indexing: `rgb corrupt' pins the EXACT post-GET value, so a
  44: #              destination receives anything other than a value distinct from BOTH the source and
  45: #              the pre-painted destination.  Restoring `exit(1)' there kills the machine and reds
  46: #              fix that merely changes the value without correcting the byte positions still reds.
  47: #              ONLY if the pre-paint is removed -- which is why the destination is PRE-PAINTED with a
  48: #              known value and the row rejects it: "left alone" and "wrote random" are then distinct.
  49: #    DOES NOT  establish that any real driver selects LIN_B.  It appears only in crmfbreg.h and the
  50: #              function's own comment; the PROM's MTE zero-fill path does not call this function; and
  51: #              there is no O2/sgimips image in the tree to boot.  **Documented and storable is NOT
  52: #              driver-selected**, and this probe claims only the former (grok, the witness panel).
  53: #
  54: #  DESIGN CREDIT AND THE FALSE NEGATIVE IT AVOIDS (grok, panel_20260906_224008).  The obvious probe --
  55: #  set the source mode to LIN_B, draw, read the destination -- FINDS NOTHING.  At reset the
  56: #  destination mode is TLB_A and re_tlb_a[] has bit 31 clear, so the PUT returns WITHOUT WRITING and
  57: #  the destination is unchanged: a false negative that looks like a clean run.  Using LIN_A as the
  58: #  DESTINATION closes it, because case 4 forces the valid bit:
  59: #      tileptr = 0x80000000 | (d->re_linear_a[x >> 12] << 12);  ofs = x & 4095;
  60: #  so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10 puts the tile at physical 0x10000, and the byte offset is x itself.
  61: #
  62: #  THE ORACLE IS CERTAIN, NOT PROBABILISTIC.  glibc random() is 31-bit, so the value written has bit
  63: #  31 CLEAR and can never equal a source of 0xAABBCCDD.  Inequality is guaranteed, not 2^-32.
  64: #

 110: #  is 0 here, so `linear = tlb_mode > 3' is FALSE -- which is the whole point.  Every other row in
 111: #  this file drives LIN_A (tlb_mode 4), so `linear' was TRUE in all of them and a repair conditioned

 113: RE_TLB_A   = 0xb5001000           # CRIME_RE_TLB_A 0x1000, same base as RE_LINEAR_A's 0x1700
 114: TLBA_TILE  = 0x8002               # re_tlb_a[0].  *** BIT 15 IS NOT DECORATION. ***
 115: TLBA_PHYS  = (TLBA_TILE << 16) & ~0x80000000   # -> 0x20000
 116: #  *** A TILED TILE POINTER MUST CARRY ITS OWN VALID BIT, AND LIN_A'S DOES NOT TELL YOU THAT. ***
 117: #  The helper gates on it before using tileptr at all:
 118: #
 119: #      // The highest bit seems to be set for a "valid" tile pointer.
 120: #      if (!(tileptr & 0x80000000))
 121: #              return;                       <- silent decline
 122: #      tileptr &= ~0x80000000;
 123: #
 124: #  LIN_A sets the bit EXPLICITLY -- `tileptr = 0x80000000 | (re_linear_a[n] << 12)' -- so a probe
 125: #  built from the linear path never has to know.  TLB_A takes it from the STORED VALUE:
 126: #  `tileptr = re_tlb_a[n] << 16', and re_tlb_a is uint16_t, so bit 31 of tileptr is bit 15 of the
 127: #  register.  `re_tlb_a[0] = 2' yields 0x00020000, the guard rejects it, and the GET returns without
 128: #  touching *color -- which leaves the caller's `fg' behind.
 129: #
 130: #  MEASURED TWICE: the row read 0x77665544 = FG_VAL, which this probe's own DE_FG comment names as
 131: #  what a declined GET leaves.  The first correction (a halfword-vs-word shift) was a real bug and
 132: #  fixed a real thing; it was not THIS thing, and the row said so again.  **Two wrong guesses, two
 133: #  identical red rows, and the value never once looked like a tiled pixel.**
 134: #  Tiled addressing is NOT the linear formula: `ofs = 512*y + (x % tilewidth)*bufdepth' rather than
 135: #  `ofs = x & 4095'.  With a 1-wide, 4-tall rectangle the four source rows are 512 bytes apart, so
 136: #  all four are painted -- unlike the LIN_A rows, which collide on one byte by design.
 137: TLBA_ROWS  = [0xa0000000 + TLBA_PHYS + 512 * i for i in range(4)]
 138: #  The SAME colour type at a DIFFERENT TRANSFER WIDTH.  bufdepth = 1 << ((mode>>8)&3),
 139: #  so 0x300 gives 8 where 0x200 gives 4.  buf[0..2] are filled either way, so the expected
 140: #  value is unchanged -- which is exactly what makes it a clean second width to test.

 143: #  WAS.  That row would have gone RED against a CORRECT fix. ***
 144: #
 145: #  RGB is a THREE-channel mode.  A corrected GET reads buf[0..2] and leaves the low byte of
 146: #  *color at zero -- there is no fourth channel to carry, and PUT's own `buf[3] = 0' says so
 147: #  from the other side.  So a source word 0xaabbccdd copies as:
 148: #
 149: #      source memory      aa bb cc dd
 150: #      GET RGB (fixed)    (aa<<24)+(bb<<16)+(cc<<8)  =  0xaabbcc00      <- dd is DROPPED
 151: #      PUT RGBA           aa bb cc 00
 152: #      destination        0xaabbcc00
 153: #
 154: #  Caught on pass 1 by FOUR seats independently naming 0xaabbcc00 -- grok, glm, the kimi
 155: #  substitute and codex -- against a brief that had stated 0xaabbccdd.  **The dropped byte is
 156: #  CORRECT BEHAVIOUR, not residual corruption**, which is exactly the distinction a row
 157: #  asserting the source value could never draw.
 158: DRAWMODE   = 0x002000f8           # XFER_EN 0x200000 | PLANEMASK 0x80 | BYTEMASK 0x78
 159: PRIM_RECT  = 0x03020000           # DE_PRIM_RECTANGLE 0x03000000 | DE_PRIM_TB 0x00020000
 160: 
 161: TILE_PHYS  = 0x10000              # re_linear_a[0] = 0x10 -> (0x10 << 12)
 162: DST_KSEG1  = 0xa0000000 + TILE_PHYS          # x = 0    -> ofs 0
 163: SRC_OFS    = 0x40
 164: SRC_KSEG1  = 0xa0000000 + TILE_PHYS + SRC_OFS  # x = 0x40 -> ofs 0x40
 165: SRC_VAL    = 0xaabbccdd           # bit 31 SET: random() (31-bit) can never equal it
 166: DST_PAINT  = 0x11111111           # pre-paint, so "left untouched" is distinguishable
 167: FG_VAL     = 0x77665544           # DISTINCTIVE and NON-ZERO on purpose: with fg = 0 the rows
 168:                                   # "left *color untouched" and "zeroed *color" are the SAME
 169:                                   # observation and the oracle is vacuous by construction.
 170: 
 171: OP_NOP      = 0x00000000
 172: OP_B_SELF   = 0x1000ffff
 173: OP_SW_T0_A3 = 0xace80000          # sw $t0, 0($a3)
 174: OP_SD_T0_A3 = 0xfce80000          # sd $t0, 0($a3)
 175: OP_LW_V0_AT = 0x8c220000          # lw $v0, 0($at)
 315: 
 316: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the accept control
 317: #  Both modes LIN_A: the copy is the operation the defect corrupts.  If this row does not
 318: #  land the source value, the probe has not driven the engine and the defect row below is
 319: #  VACUOUS -- which is why it is a row and not an assumption.
 320: A = Addr(CODE)
 321: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_LIN_A) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 322: row("copy lin_a", printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died", "0x%x" % SRC_VAL,
 323:     "ACCEPT: LIN_A source copies to the destination")
 324: 
 325: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the defect
 326: #  Source RGB, destination RGBA.  The XFER GET decodes the source with the RGB arm and the PUT
 327: #  encodes it with the RGBA arm, so the destination shows exactly what the RGB GET produced.
 328: #
 329: #  *** THESE ROWS ARE WRITTEN RED-NOW / GREEN-AFTER, WHICH IS THE ONLY SHAPE A WITNESS MAY SHIP IN.
 330: #  An earlier draft had them the other way round -- asserting the CORRUPT value, so both would have
 331: #  gone RED the moment the fix landed.  That is a fine one-shot demonstration and an unshippable
 332: #  gate row, and the ladder says so in clause (i): a witness shows the symptom on the pre-fix
 333: #  committed HEAD AND GOES GREEN POST-FIX WITH THE SAME SCRIPT.  The exact pre-fix value is not
 334: #  lost by the inversion -- it is pinned by `rgb not corrupt' below and printed in the GOT column
 335: #  of every run, so the pre-fix log records it verbatim without any row having to expect it. ***
 336: A = Addr(CODE)
 337: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 338: got_rgb = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 339: 
 340: #  THE FIX'S SUCCESS CRITERION.  RED on the committed build (it reads 0xbbccdd00), GREEN once
 341: #  GET RGB reads buf[0..2].  It pins the EXACT post-fix value rather than merely "not the corrupt
 342: #  one", so a fix that moves the bytes to some THIRD wrong place still reds.
 343: row("rgb chan", got_rgb, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 344:     "R,G,B must survive at their own byte offsets; the pad is not carried")
 345: 
 346: #  FLOOR CLAUSE (b): reds if the removed defect is reinstated VERBATIM.  It is not redundant with
 347: #  the row above -- that one fails for ANY wrong value, this one names the SPECIFIC wrong value, so
 348: #  a future edit that reintroduces the off-by-one is distinguishable from one that breaks the copy
 349: #  some other way.  Both are needed and neither implies the other.
 350: row("rgb not corrupt", got_rgb, "0x%x" % RGB_CORRUPT,
 351:     "must not be the buf[1..3] shift: 0xaabbccdd -> 0xbbccdd00", negate=True)
 352: 
 353: row("rgb alive", "alive" if alive else "died", "alive",
 354:     "the host survives: this is a wrong-value defect, not a kill")
 355: 
 356: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- same-mode RGB -> RGB
 357: #  *** ASKED FOR INDEPENDENTLY BY TWO PASS-1 SEATS, and the reason is coverage this file did not
 358: #  have: every row above is an RGB->RGBA copy, so the RGB *PUT* arm is never executed. ***
 359: #      grok: "pin both with a same-mode RT row AND a raw-memory GET whose byte 3 is nonzero"
 360: #      glm:  "a same-mode RGB->RGB copy, raw-seeded -- the most guest-realistic corruption
 361: #             (today every pixel word shifts left 8) and the natural detector row; S4 is
 362: #             RGB->RGBA only"
 363: #
 364: #  It is also the shape a real guest takes, which the mixed-mode row is not: a driver that has
 365: #  programmed RGB does not usually change the destination type mid-copy.
 366: #
 367: #  THE VALUES ARE THE SAME AS THE MIXED-MODE ROW, AND THAT IS NOT REDUNDANCY.  PUT RGB writes the
 368: #  top three bytes of whatever GET produced and zeroes buf[3], so the destination word matches the
 369: #  RGBA case -- but the code path executed is different, so a mutant in the RGB PUT arm reds HERE
 370: #  and is invisible to every row above.
 371: #
 372: #  grok's "byte 3 nonzero" condition is met by SRC_VAL itself: 0xaabbccdd has 0xdd at byte 3, so
 373: #  this row additionally proves the pad is NOT carried rather than merely being zero already.
 374: A = Addr(CODE)
 375: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB, dst_mode=MODE_RGB) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 376: got_same = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 377: 
 378: row("rgb samemode", got_same, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 379:     "RGB->RGB: exercises the RGB PUT arm, which no other row reaches")
 380: 
 381: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the WIDTH axis
 383: # 384: #  Table 1 built the canonical clause-(d) mutant -- guard KEPT, repair CONDITIONED on a field the
 385: #  guarded body reads:
 386: #
 387: #      case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 388: #              if (bufdepth == 4)
 389: #                      *color = (buf[0] << 24) + (buf[1] << 16) + (buf[2] << 8);   /* the fix */
 390: #              else
 391: #                      *color = (buf[1] << 24) + (buf[2] << 16) + (buf[3] << 8);   /* the defect */
 392: #
 393: #  and it scored a CLEAN SWEEP, 6/6, against every row above -- because EVERY session in this file
 394: #  used BUFDEPTH_32, so bufdepth is 4 in all of them and the mutant's fix branch is the only one the
 395: #  detector ever executes.  **The detector proved the fix works exactly where it was measured and
 396: #  said nothing about anywhere else.**
 397: #
 398: #  `bufdepth' is squarely inside the DEPENDS-ON closure: it is read by the guarded body (it is
 399: #  memory_rw's transfer length) and it comes from bits 8-9 of the SAME mode word whose bits 4-7 the
 400: #  guard reads.  So this is not an exotic mutant -- it is the shape the closure enumerates first.
 401: #
 402: #  Driving a SECOND width is what makes the width axis observable at all.  bufdepth 8 keeps
 403: #  buf[0..2] filled by memory_rw, so the expected value is unchanged and the row stays a value
 404: #  oracle; bufdepth 1 or 2 would leave buf[1..2] uninitialised, which is `sgirebufuninit' and would
 405: #  make this row non-deterministic -- so 8 is the only other width that can carry a value oracle.
 406: A = Addr(CODE)
 407: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB64, stepx=8) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 408: got_d64 = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 409: 
 410: row("rgb depth64", got_d64, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 411:     "the fix must hold at bufdepth 8, not only at the width the other rows use")
 412: 
 413: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the ADDRESSING-MODE axis
 414: #  *** THIS ROW CLOSES A SECOND MEASURED CLAUSE-(d) FLOOR BREACH, found by pass 2. ***
 415: #
 416: #  Mutant M12_linear -- guard kept, repair conditioned on `linear':
 417: #
 418: #      case DE_MODE_TYPE_RGB:
 419: #              if (linear) *color = (buf[0]<<24)+(buf[1]<<16)+(buf[2]<<8);   /* the fix    */
 420: #              else        *color = (buf[1]<<24)+(buf[2]<<16)+(buf[3]<<8);   /* the defect */
 421: #
 422: #  scored **8/8, a CLEAN SWEEP**, because every row above drives LIN_A (tlb_mode 4) and `linear' is
 423: #  therefore true in all of them.  `linear' is `tlb_mode > 3' and it DOMINATES the edited lines via
 424: #  the `if (!linear && (x < 0 || ...)) return;' guard, so it sits inside the DEPENDS-ON closure --
 425: #  this is a floor breach, not coverage debt.
 426: #
 427: #  The seat that named the axis proposed conditioning on `tileptr'.  That cannot work as stated:
 428: #  `tileptr &= ~0x80000000' strips the linear flag BEFORE the switch, so `tileptr' carries no
 429: #  addressing-mode information by the time the arm runs.  `linear' is the variable that survives, and
 430: #  building the mutant in the form the source permits is what turned a prediction into a measurement.
 431: #
 432: #  THE SOURCE is tiled and the DESTINATION stays LIN_A, deliberately: the RGB arm under test is the
 433: #  GET, and the GET's `linear' comes from src_mode.  A tiled DESTINATION would exercise the PUT and
 434: #  leave this mutant untouched.
 435: A = Addr(CODE)
 436: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB_TLBA, tiled_src=True) + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 437: got_tiled = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 438: 
 439: row("rgb tiled", got_tiled, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 440:     "the fix must hold for a TILED source, not only for LIN_A")
 441: 
 442: # ---------------------------------------------------------------- the JOINT cell
 443: #  *** THIS ROW CLOSES THE CROSS-PRODUCT, AND IT EXISTS BECAUSE I REPAIRED CELL-BY-CELL TWICE. ***
 444: #
 445: #  Two axes were each closed on their own:  WIDTH by `rgb depth64' (LIN_A, bufdepth 8) and
 446: #  ADDRESSING by `rgb tiled' (tiled, bufdepth 4).  That leaves a 2x2 with one corner empty:
 447: #
 448: #                     bufdepth 4                  bufdepth 8
 449: #      LIN_A          rgb chan / rgb samemode     rgb depth64
 450: #      tiled          rgb tiled                   *** was UNROWED ***
 451: #
 452: #  and a repair conditioned on `(bufdepth == 4 || linear)' is TRUE in all three rowed cells, so it
 453: #  scores 9/9 BY CONSTRUCTION.  Three pass-3 seats named that cell independently -- grok, the kimi
 454: #  substitute and deepseek -- and the kimi seat wrote the mutant out in exactly that form.
 455: #
 456: #  The carrier's class-closure amendment is explicit about the remedy: when two consecutive passes
 457: #  find breaches of ONE CLASS, the next repair owes the CLASS CLOSURE -- enumerate the full space and
 458: #  close every cell in ONE diff, rather than adding the cell the latest mutant happened to name.
 459: #  **Repairing at exactly the address the mutant used is what produced breaches two and three.**
 460: #
 461: #  Geometry check for bufdepth 8 under tiled addressing: tilewidth_in_pixels = 512/8 = 64, so
 462: #  tile_nr = (y>>7)*16 + x/64 = 0 for x=0, y=0..3, and ofs = 512*y + (x % 64)*8 = 512*y -- the same
 463: #  four source rows TLBA_ROWS already paints.  No new setup is needed, only the mode and the step.
 464: A = Addr(CODE)
 465: t, alive = session(setup(A, MODE_RGB_TLBA64, tiled_src=True, stepx=8)
 466:                    + load(A, DST_KSEG1) + ["print v0"])
 467: got_t64 = printed(t, "v0") if alive else "died"
 468: 
 469: row("rgb tiled64", got_t64, "0x%x" % RGB_FIXED,
 470:     "the JOINT cell: tiled source AND bufdepth 8, which no other row reaches")
 471: 
 472: ok = sum(1 for _, o in rows if o)
 473: print("")
 474: print("SGIRGB_RESULT=%d/%d" % (ok, len(rows)))
 475: print("SGIRGB_WITNESS=%s" % ("PASS" if ok == len(rows) else "FAIL"))
 476: print("SGIRGB_DONE")
 477: sys.exit(0 if ok == len(rows) else 1)

I also found a sixth inequality row, `rgb not corrupt`, that still passes on missing output. Its equality sibling rejects that state, but the row’s own claim needs correction.

 190: #  ALPHA_REG_NAMES calls register 3 "t2" -- an earlier draft printed "t1" (register 2), which

 207: #  cpu->running = 0 BEFORE its switch, so the handler never runs.  This row proves we are on the

 221: #  DEBUGGER, because -V single-steps -- the emulator does not exit.  So this row cannot distinguish

 185: def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.6):
 186:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
 187:     if pid == 0:
 188:         os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64",

 192:     def rd(t):
 193:         nonlocal buf
 194:         end = time.time() + t
 195:         while time.time() < end:
 196:             r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
 197:             if not r:
 198:                 continue
 199:             try:
 200:                 d = os.read(fd, 65536)
 201:                 if not d:
 202:                     return False
 203:                 buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
 204:             except OSError:
 205:                 return False
 206:         return True
 207:     alive = rd(settle)
 208:     for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
 209:         try:
 210:             os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
 211:         except OSError:
 212:             alive = False
 213:             break
 214:         if not rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.22):

 216:             break

 218:         os.write(fd, b"quit\n"); rd(0.4)

 227: def printed(t, reg):
 228:     #  MASK TO 32 BITS.  `lw' sign-extends into a 64-bit register, so a value with bit 31 set
 229:     #  reads back as 0xffffffffaabbccdd and a row comparing raw strings fails on FORMATTING
 230:     #  rather than on behaviour -- measured on this probe's first run.  The shipped
 231:     #  sgi_re_width_probe.py carries the same normalisation for the same reason.
 232:     m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
 233:     return "absent" if not m else "0x%x" % (int(m.group(1), 16) & 0xffffffff)

 235: class Addr(object):
 236:     def __init__(self, base): self.a = base
 237:     def take(self, n):
 238:         got = self.a; self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20; return got

 240: def run_ops(ops, at):
 241:     put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
 242:     put += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
 243:             "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP)]

 246: def store(A, addr, val, op=OP_SW_T0_A3):

 253: def setup(A, src_mode, dst_mode=None, stepx=4, tiled_src=False):
 254:     """Program the engine, paint source and destination, then fire one rectangle.
 255:        Every store is a REAL GUEST INSTRUCTION through memory_rw -- no device_add,
 256:        no debugger `put' to a device register."""
 257:     #  *** THE 64-BIT STORE WRITES THE HIGH WORD TO [n] AND THE LOW WORD TO [n+1]. ***  Measured:
 258:     #  `sd' of 0x10 gave `re_linear_a[0] = 0, [1] = 0x10', so the tile pointer was 0 and every pixel
 259:     #  went to PHYSICAL ADDRESS 0 -- the destination stayed at its pre-paint and the ACCEPT control
 260:     #  failed, which is precisely the "do not leave it 0: that writes phys 0" trap grok named.  The
 261:     #  value below puts 0x10 in the HIGH word so re_linear_a[0] = 0x10.
 262:     c  = store(A, RE_LINEAR_A, 0x0000001000000000, op=OP_SD_T0_A3)  # sd: a 4-byte sw is DECLINED here
 263:     c += store(A, DST_KSEG1, DST_PAINT)
 264:     c += store(A, SRC_KSEG1, SRC_VAL)
 265:     if tiled_src:
 266:         #  *** re_tlb_a IS uint16_t[256] AND ITS STORE LOOP IS PER-HALFWORD, WHICH IS NOT
 267:         #  RE_LINEAR_A'S SHAPE.  Getting this wrong is what the row caught on its first run. ***
 268:         #      for (hwi = 0; hwi < len; hwi += 2)
 269:         #              d->re_tlb_a[tlbi] = data[hwi]*256 + data[hwi+1];
 270:         #  So an 8-byte store fills re_tlb_a[0..3] from FOUR big-endian halfwords, and the one
 271:         #  that lands in [0] is bits 63..48.  `TLBA_TILE << 32' -- the shift RE_LINEAR_A needs,
 272:         #  because re_linear_a is uint32_t -- puts the value in re_tlb_a[2] and leaves [0] at
 273:         #  zero, giving tileptr 0 and a DECLINED get.  Measured: the row read FG_VAL
 274:         #  (0x77665544), which this probe's own DE_FG comment names as what a declined GET
 275:         #  leaves.  **The row reported my setup error instead of passing quietly.**
 276:         c += store(A, RE_TLB_A, TLBA_TILE << 48, op=OP_SD_T0_A3)
 277:         for a in TLBA_ROWS:
 278:             c += store(A, a, SRC_VAL)
 279:     c += store(A, DE_MODE_DST,   MODE_LIN_A if dst_mode is None else dst_mode)
 280:     c += store(A, DE_MODE_SRC,   src_mode)
 281:     c += store(A, DE_DRAWMODE,   DRAWMODE)
 282:     c += store(A, DE_XFER_ADDR,  0 if tiled_src else SRC_OFS)   # linear: src_x = addr_src (a BYTE
 283:                                                       # offset).  Tiled: src_x is a PIXEL
 284:                                                       # coordinate, so 0 is the tile's origin.
 285:     c += store(A, DE_XFER_STEPX, stepx)                # must equal src bufdepth or the XFER arm warns
 286:     c += store(A, DE_FG,         FG_VAL)               # what a declined GET leaves in `color'
 287:     c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_0,  0x00000000)           # (x1,y1) = (0,0)
 288:     #  *** (x2,y2) = (0,3): ONE COLUMN, FOUR ROWS.  The height is load-bearing and the width must
 289:     #  NOT be used instead. ***  (The latch row that needed the height lives in the probe this
 290:     #  file was copied from, not here -- but the geometry is kept so the two sessions stay
 291:     #  comparable, and because LIN_A addressing is `ofs = x & 4095' either way.
 292:     #  is the announcement COUNT -- which cannot vary at all if the rectangle is one pixel, because an
 293:     #  unlatched announce then also fires exactly once.  Measured: with (0,0)-(0,0) the M_NOLATCH
 294:     #  mutant scored 8/8, a clean escape, and the row was VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION.
 295:     #  It is the HEIGHT and not the width because LIN_A addressing is `ofs = x & 4095' -- y is
 296:     #  ignored -- so four ROWS write the same byte four times (the value oracle survives) while four
 297:     #  COLUMNS would write overlapping bytes at ofs 0..3 and destroy it.
 298:     c += store(A, DE_XVERTEX_1,  0x00000003)           # (x2,y2) = (0,3): 4 pixels, all at ofs 0
 299:     c += store(A, DE_PRIM_START, PRIM_RECT)            # PRIMITIVE|START fires the draw
 300:     return c

 360: 				/*  debug("OK MAP 1, i=%i { vaddr=%016" PRIx64" "
 361: 				    "==> paddr %016" PRIx64" v=%i d=%i "
 362: 				    "asid=0x%02x }\n", i, (uint64_t) vaddr,
 363: 				    (uint64_t) *return_paddr, v_bit?1:0,
  35:  *  arg[2] = offset (as an int32_t)
  36:  *
  37:  *  NOTE:
  38:  *	Alpha byte and word loads (8- and 16-bit) are unsigned, while
  39:  *	32-bit long words are sign-extended up to 64 bits during a load!
  40:  */
  41: 
  42: 
  43: #ifndef LS_IGNORE_OFFSET
  44: static void LS_GENERIC_N(struct cpu *cpu, struct alpha_instr_call *ic)
  45: {
  46: #ifdef LS_B
  47: 	unsigned char data[1];
  48: #endif
  49: #ifdef LS_W
  50: 	unsigned char data[2];
  51: #endif
  52: #ifdef LS_L
  53: 	unsigned char data[4];
  54: #endif
  55: #ifdef LS_Q
  56: 	unsigned char data[8];
  57: #endif
  58: 	uint64_t addr = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[1]);
  59: 	uint64_t data_x;
  60: 
  61: 	addr += (int32_t)ic->arg[2];
  62: #ifdef LS_UNALIGNED
  63: 	addr &= ~7;
  64: #endif
  65: 
  66: #ifdef LS_LOAD
  67: 	/*  Load:  */
  68: 	if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, data, sizeof(data),
  69: 	    MEM_READ, CACHE_DATA)) {
  70: 		/*  #215: (Codex/Fable) memory_rw() returned 0 = the translator
  71: 		    already signalled a fault (cf. #194's no-translation return);
  72: 		    abandon the access instead of exit()ing the host.  */
  73: 		return;
  74: 	}
  75: 
  76: 	data_x = data[0];
  77: #ifndef LS_B
  78: 	data_x += (data[1] << 8);
  79: #ifndef LS_W
  80: 	data_x += (data[2] << 16);
  81: 	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[3] << 24);
  82: #ifdef LS_L
  83: 	data_x = (int64_t)(int32_t)data_x;
  84: #endif
  85: #ifndef LS_L
  86: 	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[4] << 32);
  87: 	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[5] << 40);
  88: 	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[6] << 48);
  89: 	data_x += ((uint64_t)data[7] << 56);
  90: #endif
  91: #endif
  92: #endif
  93: 	*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = data_x;
  94: #else
  95: 	/*  Store:  */
  96: 	data_x = *((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]);
  97: 	data[0] = data_x;
  98: #ifndef LS_B
  99: 	data[1] = data_x >> 8;
 100: #ifndef LS_W
 101: 	data[2] = data_x >> 16;
 102: 	data[3] = data_x >> 24;
 103: #ifndef LS_L
 104: 	data[4] = data_x >> 32;
 105: 	data[5] = data_x >> 40;
 106: 	data[6] = data_x >> 48;
 107: 	data[7] = data_x >> 56;
 108: #endif
 109: #endif
 110: #endif
 111: 
 112: 	if (!cpu->memory_rw(cpu, cpu->mem, addr, data, sizeof(data),
 113: 	    MEM_WRITE, CACHE_DATA)) {
 114: 		/*  #215: (Codex/Fable) memory_rw() returned 0 = the translator
 115: 		    already signalled a fault (cf. #194's no-translation return);
 116: 		    abandon the access instead of exit()ing the host.  */
 117: 		return;
 118: 	}
 119: 
 120: #ifdef LS_LLSC
 121: #ifndef LS_LOAD
 122: 	*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = 1;
 123: #endif
 124: #endif
 125: 
 126: #endif
 127: }
 128: #endif
 129: 
 130: 
 131: static void LS_N(struct cpu *cpu, struct alpha_instr_call *ic)
 132: {
 133: 	unsigned char *page;
 134: 	uint64_t addr = (*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[1]))
 135: #ifndef LS_IGNORE_OFFSET
 136: 	    + (int32_t)ic->arg[2]
 137: #endif
 138: 	    ;
 139: 
 140: 	const uint32_t mask1 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L1N) - 1;
 141: 	const uint32_t mask2 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L2N) - 1;
 142: 	const uint32_t mask3 = (1 << DYNTRANS_L3N) - 1;
 143: 	uint32_t x1, x2, x3, c;
 144: 	struct DYNTRANS_L2_64_TABLE *l2;
 145: 	struct DYNTRANS_L3_64_TABLE *l3;
 146: 	x1 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N)) & mask1;
 147: 	x2 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N)) & mask2;
 148: 	x3 = (addr >> (64-DYNTRANS_L1N-DYNTRANS_L2N-DYNTRANS_L3N)) & mask3;
 149: 	/*  fatal("X3: addr=%016" PRIx64" x1=%x x2=%x x3=%x\n",
 150: 	    (uint64_t) addr, (int) x1, (int) x2, (int) x3);  */
 151: 	l2 = cpu->cd.DYNTRANS_ARCH.l1_64[x1];
 152: 	/*  fatal("  l2 = %p\n", l2);  */
 153: 	l3 = l2->l3[x2];
 154: 	/*  fatal("  l3 = %p\n", l3);  */
 155: #ifdef LS_LOAD
 156: 	page = l3->host_load[x3];
 157: #else
 158: 	page = l3->host_store[x3];
 159: #endif
 160: 
 161: #ifdef LS_UNALIGNED
 162: 	addr &= ~7;
 163: #endif
 164: 
 165: #ifdef LS_LLSC
 166: #ifdef LS_LOAD
 167: 	/*  TODO: cache-line size!  */
 168: 	cpu->cd.alpha.load_linked_addr = addr & ~63;
 169: 	cpu->cd.alpha.ll_flag = 1;
 170: #else
 171: 	/*  TODO: only invalidate per cache line, not everything!  */
 172: 	if (cpu->cd.alpha.ll_flag == 1) {
 173: 		int i;
 174: 		for (i=0; i<cpu->machine->ncpus; i++)
 175: 			cpu->machine->cpus[i]->cd.alpha.ll_flag = 0;
 176: 	} else {
 177: 		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = 0;
 178: 		return;
 179: 	}
 180: #endif
 181: #endif
 182: 
 183: 	c = addr & 8191;
 184: 
 185: #ifndef LS_B
 186: 	if (c &
 187: #ifdef LS_W
 188: 	    1
 189: #endif
 190: #ifdef LS_L
 191: 	    3
 192: #endif
 193: #ifdef LS_Q
 194: 	    7
 195: #endif
 196: 	    ) {
 197: 		LS_GENERIC_N(cpu, ic);
 198: 		return;
 199: 	}
 200: 	else
 201: #endif
 202: 
 203: 	if (page != NULL) {
 204: #ifdef LS_LOAD
 205: #ifdef HOST_BIG_ENDIAN
 206: 		uint64_t data_x;
 207: 		data_x = page[c];
 208: #ifndef LS_B
 209: 		data_x += (page[c+1] << 8);
 210: #ifndef LS_W
 211: 		data_x += (page[c+2] << 16);
 212: 		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+3] << 24);
 213: #ifndef LS_L
 214: 		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+4] << 32);
 215: 		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+5] << 40);
 216: 		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+6] << 48);
 217: 		data_x += ((uint64_t)page[c+7] << 56);
 218: #endif
 219: #endif
 220: #endif
 221: #ifdef LS_L
 222: 		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = (int64_t)(int32_t)data_x;
 223: #else
 224: 		*((uint64_t *)ic->arg[0]) = data_x;
 225: #endif
  63:  *  (MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED is 0.)
  64:  */
  65: 
  66: /*
  67:  *  #430: THE PAGE SIZE, IN ONE PLACE.
  68:  *
  69:  *  It was a local inside the function.  The wrapper below needs the same number, and two
  70:  *  copies of a page size that can silently diverge is the shape this tree has been bitten
  71:  *  by before -- so it is hoisted to a macro and the local now reads it.  Note the Alpha
  72:  *  value: it is 8 KB, not 4 KB, so anything that splits at "the page boundary" must use
  73:  *  THIS and never a literal 4096.  A review seat caught exactly that hazard in the design
  74:  *  brief, which had said "4 KB page" throughout.
  75:  */
  76: #ifdef MEM_ALPHA
  77: #define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0x1fff
  78: #else
  79: #define	MRW_OFFSET_MASK		0xfff
  80: #endif
  81: 
  82: /*
  83:  *  #430: THE SPLIT GRANULE IS 1 KB, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME NUMBER AS THE MASK ABOVE.
  84:  *
  85:  *  MRW_OFFSET_MASK is the HOST-page granule: what memory_paddr_to_hostaddr() is asked for
  86:  *  and what update_translation_table() caches.  The granule the SPLIT needs is a different
  87:  *  thing -- the smallest unit at which a translator in this tree can send two adjacent
  88:  *  virtual addresses to unrelated physical ones -- and it is SMALLER:
  89:  *
  90:  *      memory_arm.c:198-207   descriptor type 3 on a non-XScale core is a 1 KB page,
  91:  *                             *return_paddr = (d2 & 0xfffffc00) | (vaddr & 0x3ff)
  92:  *      memory_sh.c:116        SH4_PTEL_SZ_1K gives mask 0xfffffc00
  93:  *      memory_mips_v2p.c      the VR41xx path likewise has 1 KB logic
  94:  *
  95:  *  A first draft of this split used the 4 KB mask and a review seat produced the
  96:  *  counterexample: with a 1 KB mapping, a four-byte access at 0x13fe crosses a translation
  97:  *  boundary but NOT a 4 KB one, so the loop never fired and the defect survived exactly
  98:  *  where MEMORY_NOT_FULL_PAGE says it is most likely.  Splitting finer is always SAFE --
  99:  *  each chunk is then inside one mapping for every translator here, and the worker's own
 100:  *  host-page arithmetic is unchanged -- so the only cost is more worker calls on the slow
 101:  *  path for a multi-page translated access, which is rare (large transfers are PHYSICAL
 102:  *  and exempt).  Correctness at a known granule beats a faster one that is wrong on ARM.
 103:  */
 104: #define	MRW_SPLIT_MASK		0x3ff
 105: 
 106: /*  The one-page worker; its name is derived from MEMORY_RW because cpu_alpha.c includes
 107:     this file TWICE in one translation unit (alpha_userland_memory_rw, then alpha_memory_rw
 108:     via tmp_alpha_tail.c), so a plainly named static would be a redefinition.  */
 109: #ifndef MRW_CAT2
 110: #define	MRW_CAT2(a,b)		a##b
 111: #define	MRW_CAT(a,b)		MRW_CAT2(a,b)
 112: #endif
 113: #define	MEMORY_RW_ONE_PAGE	MRW_CAT(MEMORY_RW, _one_page)
 114: 
 115: /*
 116:  *  The one relationship between the two that MUST hold, asserted at COMPILE TIME because it
 117:  *  cannot be asserted at run time by the offline differential.
 118:  *
 119:  *  MEM_ALPHA is never compiled by that differential -- its fixture is 4 KB-specific -- so a
 120:  *  review seat MEASURED that widening MRW_OFFSET_MASK, or hardcoding the worker's local back
 121:  *  to 0xfff (exactly the drift this macro exists to prevent), leaves every row green.  A
rg: GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugger*: IO error for operation on GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugger*: The filename, directory name, or volume label syntax is incorrect. (os error 123)

    src = subprocess.check_output(['git','-C',str(repo),'show','7a41b3f:'+path], text=True)
    tree = ast.parse(src)
    row_def = next(n for n in tree.body if isinstance(n, ast.FunctionDef) and n.name == 'row')
    ns = {'rows':[], 'print':lambda *a, **k:None}
    exec(compile(ast.Module(body=[row_def], type_ignores=[]),path,'exec'),ns)
    calls = [n for n in ast.walk(tree) if isinstance(n,ast.Call) and isinstance(n.func,ast.Name) and n.func.id=='row']
    print(path, 'total rows:',len(calls))
    for call in sorted(calls,key=lambda n:n.lineno):
        if not any(k.arg=='negate' and isinstance(k.value,ast.Constant) and k.value.value is True for k in call.keywords): continue
        name = ast.literal_eval(call.args[0])
        want = eval(compile(ast.Expression(call.args[2]),path,'eval'), {'__builtins__':{}}, constants)
        vals = [ns['row'](name, state, want, negate=True) for state in ['absent','died','0x0']]
        print(' ',call.lineno,name,'want "'!='"',want,*['GREEN' if v else 'RED' for v in vals])

def read_git(*args):
    return subprocess.check_output(['git','-C','GXEMUL-SEC',*args]).decode('utf-8','replace')

print('Original updates:',updates)

print('LCA guard/cast enumeration on the stated truncating 32-bit-int implementation')
for value in [0,1,2,0x100000000,0x100000001]:
    guarded = value & ((1<<64)-2)
    narrowed = value & 0xffffffff
    print(hex(value), 'guard =',hex(guarded),'old shown =',hex(narrowed),'new shown =',hex(value))

base,va=0x105c000,0x3008

The isolated predicate checks confirm that none of the tested non-observation states can make an affected script pass all rows: the equality sibling rejects each one. Partial scores and individual control claims remain overstated. The three non-RGB fixes match their named defects; the RGB index change is correct, but it leaves signed-shift UB in the expression it repairs.

VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED. I would accept #506, #507 and #508 as scoped repairs. I would have held #505 until its edited expression used unsigned shifts. All four named defects are real, but some evidence and comment claims are wrong.

I reviewed `GXEMUL-SEC/src/`, the committed witness scripts, and their subsequent working-tree repairs. I ran isolated predicate checks and arithmetic enumerations, without running an emulator or gate.

1. **#505: correct byte positions, incomplete correction.** The independent consumer supports `[R,G,B,pad]`: [GBE scanout](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_gbe.c:288) assembles the bytes, and its RGB8 decoder takes channels from bits 31–24, 23–16 and 15–8. For raw bytes `11 22 33 44`, the old GET computes `0x22334400`; the corrected indexing computes `0x11223300`.

   But [the edited expression](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_re.c:350) still shifts a promoted signed `int`. On a C implementation with 32-bit `int`, input bytes `80 01 02 03` previously produced the defined, incorrect value `0x01020300`; the new expression evaluates undefined `128 << 24`. **The edit changes which inputs encounter UB.** A `uint32_t` destination does not fix the intermediate expression.

   I would require casts **before** shifting in this arm. The comment’s unconditional `0xaabbccdd → 0xaabbcc00` guarantee is therefore too strong, and the high-byte witnesses establish observed compiler behavior rather than defined C behavior. The cited GBE packing expression has the same signed-shift issue.

2. **#506: both formatting changes are correct.** Enumerating the guard: `0` and `1` are accepted; `0x100000000` and `0x100000001` are rejected. On the stated truncating implementation, the old diagnostics display respectively `0` and `1` for those rejected values. The replacements preserve the full values.

   The claimed five full-width sibling diagnostics really are **TB_ENA, W_BASE0, W_MASK0, W_BASE1, W_MASK1**. However, [the witness](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/lca_confwidth_probe.py:176) exercises IOC_CONF only. Its score does not establish coverage of the separately changed default-arm formatter.

3. **#507: correct field repair.** In field order `ksp, usp, ptbr, cpc, asn, unique, flags, decrsv0, decrsv1`, the original assignment counts are **1,1,2,1,1,1,0,1,1**. After the fix, each is assigned once. The save/load offsets and [PCB declaration](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/include/thirdparty/alpha_cpu.h:61) independently support PTBR at `+16` and flags at `+40`.

   Two comment corrections: the original PTBR assignments were at lines **278 and 282**, four lines apart, with three intervening lines. Also, “never-loaded” should mean **not loaded from the incoming PCB**: `wrfen` also writes `apcb_flags`, so the subsequently saved value is stale state, not necessarily zero or never assigned.

4. **#508: correct offset repair; keeping the TODO was right.** I enumerated all **8,192 offsets** for both witness data-page bases. The old result differs from the offset-preserving result at **8,191** offsets; OR and addition differ at **zero** offsets when combining these aligned bases with the correctly masked offset.

   [The consumer](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:736) confirms why the returned address needs the offset. The fast load path independently uses virtual-address offset bits, supporting the witness’s requirement for a cold access. Watchpoint registration also directly consumes the translator’s returned address.

   The added comment explicitly discloses ignored `flags`, unchecked protection bits, and writable success returns. That adequately limits this round to address calculation; it does not claim complete MMU correctness.

**Your no-escape belief holds for the enumerated non-observation states at the scripts’ own all-rows-pass criterion.** Running the committed row helpers produced:

| Original inequality row | `"absent"` | `"died"`¹ | `"0x0"` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `swpctx notflags` | GREEN | GREEN | GREEN |
| `swpctx mapped` | GREEN | GREEN | RED |
| `walk stored` | GREEN | GREEN | GREEN |
| `walk mapped` | GREEN | GREEN | RED |
| `kill notzero` | GREEN | GREEN | RED |
| `rgb not corrupt` | GREEN | GREEN | GREEN |

¹ Synthetic readback input; not every parser emits this string.

The corresponding equality rows—`swpctx ptbr`, `walk off`, `kill width`, and `rgb chan`—reject every listed state. Their scripts require `ok == len(rows)`, so these inequality defects cannot independently create an overall pass.

However:

- **There is a sixth affected row, across a fourth witness.** [RGB’s `rgb not corrupt`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/sgi_re_rgb_probe.py:350) remains unrepaired.
- Partial GREEN totals overstate successful observations. These inequalities also add no independent acceptance constraint once the corresponding exact equality passes. RGB’s comment saying neither row implies the other is false.
- A RED equality row can mean missing observation. Consequently, historical RED/GREEN labels alone do not establish that the pre-fix run observed the named defect.
- `walk found` still treats absence of an error message as proof that a valid walk occurred. Its own control description remains stronger than its predicate.

**A separate #508 witness claim is arithmetically false.** Its [comment claiming wider masks are equivalent](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/alpha_pfnoffset_probe.py:254) overlooks that the expression masks the full virtual address. For its actual second case, `vaddr = 0x3008`, physical page base `0x0105c000`:

| Mask | Masked virtual address | Returned physical address |
|---|---|---|
| `0x0fff` | `0x0008` | `0x0105c008` |
| `0x1fff` | `0x1008` | **`0x0105d008`** |
| `0x3fff` | `0x3008` | `0x0105f008` |
| `0x7fff` | `0x3008` | `0x0105f008` |

Thus literal substitutions of either wider mask should fail `walk vpn`. Reconciling any claimed survival requires the **exact mutant expressions and second-row observations**.

Two further findings:

- The product diff has **5 hunks, 57 additions and 5 deletions: 62 changed lines**, not 63. Per-file totals are **8 + 16 + 13 + 25**.
- The existing [swpctx return-register mismatch](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/cpu_alpha_palcode.c:250) remains: the comment names `v0`, but the assignment targets `a0`.

The historical measurements and subsequent bidirectional reruns remain unverified here: the packet supplies their reported outcomes, without per-row transcripts. Particularly useful missing measurements are an unsigned-shift/sanitizer check for #505, a full-width default-arm diagnostic observation for #506, and the wider-mask observations identified above.
